\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
preserves kernels and cokernels
(because it has adjoints on both sides by
Lemma
3.4
), and it is also
conservative (by Lemma
3.12
).
Hence the assertion follows from
Lemma
4.31
(a) applied
to the functor
\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}
.
Part (b) follows from
Lemma
4.31
(b).
∎

Corollary 4.33
.

(a)
Let
\mathbf{A}
be an idempotent-complete additive
DG-category with shifts and twists.
Assume that the additive category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
is abelian.
Then the additive category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
is abelian, too.

(b)
Let
\mathbf{A}
be an idempotent-complete exact DG-category
with twists.
Assume that the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
is abelian with
the abelian exact category structure.
Then the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
is also abelian with
the abelian exact category structure.

Proof.

In view of Proposition
4.32
,
it suffices to show that all kernels and cokernels exist in
the additive category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
.
Indeed, let
g\colon B\longrightarrow C
be a morphism in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
.
Then the morphism
\Phi(g)\colon\Phi(B)\longrightarrow\Phi(C)
has a kernel
in the abelian category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
.
Since the functor
\Psi^{+}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
is
a right adjoint (to
\Phi[-1]
) by
Lemma
3.4
, it follows that
the morphism
\Psi^{+}\Phi(g)
has a kernel in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
.
By Lemma
3.8
, we have
\Xi(g)\simeq\Psi^{+}\Phi(g)
;
so the morphism
\Xi(g)
has a kernel.
It remains to apply Lemma
4.6
(a) in order
to establish that the morphism
g
has a kernel in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
.
The dual argument proves the existence of cokernels.
∎

Proposition 4.34
.

(a)
Let
\mathbf{A}
be an additive DG-category with shifts
and cones.
Assume that the additive category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
is abelian.
Then the category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
is abelian, too.

(b)
Let
\mathbf{A}
be an exact DG-category.
Assume that the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
is abelian with
the abelian exact category structure.
Then the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
is also abelian with
the abelian exact category structure.

Proof.

Since all kernels and cokernels exist in the abelian category

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
,  Lemma
4.16
tells that
they also exist in the additive category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
.
The additive functor
\Psi_{\mathbf{A}}^{+}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})

preserves kernels and cokernels (because it has adjoints on both sides
by Lemma
3.4
), and it is also
conservative (by Lemma
3.12
).
Hence the assertion follows from
Lemma
4.31
(a) applied
to the functor
\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{A}}
.
Part (b) follows from part (a) and
Lemma
4.31
(b).
∎

Proposition 4.35
.

Any abelian DG-category is idempotent-complete, and all twists
exist in it.

Proof.

The first assertion holds by the definition, as any abelian category
is idempotent-complete.
The nontrivial part is the existence of twists.
The discussion in
[
32
, Section 7]
sheds some additional light
on the argument below.

Let
\mathbf{A}
be an abelian DG-category.
Then the DG-category
\mathbf{A}^{\natural}
is also abelian by
Proposition
4.34
(a), since
the additive category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
is abelian.
By Proposition
3.5
, we have a fully
faithful DG-functor
\natural\natural\colon\mathbf{A}\longrightarrow\mathbf{A}^{\natural\natural}
.
Hence the induced additive functor
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\natural\natural)\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural\natural})
is a fully faithful functor between
abelian categories.

Let us check that the functor
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\natural\natural)
is exact.
Indeed, the functor
\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})

preserves and reflects short exact sequences, and so does the
functor
\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{A}^{\natural}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural\natural})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
.
It remains to recall that the three functors
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\natural\natural)
,

\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{A}^{\natural}}
, and
\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}
form a commutative triangle
diagram of additive functors by
Lemma
3.6
.

We have shown that the essential image of the functor
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\natural\natural)

is a full subcategory closed under kernels and cokernels in the abelian
category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural\natural})
.
In order to prove that this full subcategory coincides with the whole
category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural\natural})
, it remains to check that any object
of
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural\natural})
is the cokernel of a morphism between two
objects coming from
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
.
For this purpose, it is sufficient to see that every object of

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural\natural})
is the target of an epimorphism with
the source belonging to
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
.

Indeed, for any object
X\in\mathbf{A}^{\natural\natural}
we have a natural
short exact sequence
0\longrightarrow X[-1]\longrightarrow\Xi_{\mathbf{A}^{\natural\natural}}(X)\longrightarrow X\longrightarrow 0
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural\natural})
by
Lemma
4.2
, and

\Xi_{\mathbf{A}^{\natural\natural}}(X)\simeq\Phi_{\mathbf{A}^{\natural}}\Psi^{-}_{\mathbf{A}^{\natural}}(X)\simeq\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\natural\natural)\Psi^{-}_{\mathbf{A}}\Psi^{-}_{\mathbf{A}^{\natural}}(X)

by Lemmas
3.7

and
3.8
.
So
X
is the cokernel of a natural morphism

\Xi_{\mathbf{A}^{\natural\natural}}(X)[-1]\longrightarrow\Xi_{\mathbf{A}^{\natural\natural}}(X)
in

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural\natural})
between two objects coming from
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
.

We can conclude that the functor
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\natural\natural)\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural\natural})
is an equivalence of (abelian) categories,
and it follows that the fully faithful DG-functor
\natural\natural\colon\mathbf{A}\longrightarrow\mathbf{A}^{\natural\natural}
is an equivalence of DG-categories.
Since the DG-category
\mathbf{B}^{\natural}
has twists for any DG-category

\mathbf{B}
, it follows that our DG-category
\mathbf{A}
has twists.

For a further discussion suggesting a direct constuction of twists
of objects of
\mathbf{A}
as cokernels in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
,
see
[
53
, proof of Proposition 3.9]
.
∎

Let us formulate the main point of the proof above as a separate
assertion.

Corollary 4.36
.

For any abelian DG-category
\mathbf{A}
, the DG-functor

\natural\natural\colon\mathbf{A}\longrightarrow\mathbf{A}^{\natural\natural}
from
Proposition
3.5
is an equivalence of
DG-categories.

Proof.

Follows from Propositions
3.5

and
4.35
.
∎

The following corollaries, summarizing the previous results, list
several equivalent characterizations of abelian DG-categories.

Corollary 4.37
.

For any DG-category
\mathbf{A}
with shifts, the following conditions
are equivalent:

(1)

the DG-category
\mathbf{A}
is abelian;

(2)

the DG-category
\mathbf{A}
has cones, and the preadditive
category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
is abelian;

(3)

the DG-category
\mathbf{A}
is additive, has cones, all kernels
and cokernels exists in the additive category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
, and
the additive category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
is abelian;

(4)

the DG-category
\mathbf{A}
is additive and idempotent-complete,
has all twists, and the additive category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
is
abelian.

Proof.

Follows from Propositions
4.32
(a),

4.34
(a),

4.35
, and
Corollary
4.33
(a).
∎

Corollary 4.38
.

For any exact DG-category
\mathbf{A}
, the following conditions
are equivalent:

(1)

the exact DG-category
\mathbf{A}
is abelian with the abelian
exact DG-category structure;

(2)

the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
is abelian with the abelian
exact structure;

(3)

all kernels and cokernels exist in the additive category

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
, and the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
is abelian
with the abelian exact structure;

(4)

the DG-category
\mathbf{A}
is idempotent-complete, has all twists,
and the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
is abelian with the abelian
exact structure.

Proof.

Follows from Propositions
4.32
(b),

4.34
(b),

4.35
, and
Corollary
4.33
(b).
∎

4.6.
Examples

In this section we briefly discuss the natural exact (or even abelian)
DG-category structures for our thematic examples of DG-categories
from Sections
2
and
3.4
.

Example 4.39
.

Let
\mathsf{A}
be an additive category and
\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{A})
be the DG-category
of complexes in
\mathsf{A}
.
Building up on the discussion in Example
3.16
,
we consider the additive category
\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{A})
of graded objects in
\mathsf{A}

and the additive category
\mathsf{C}(\mathsf{A})
of complexes in
\mathsf{A}
.
The forgetful functor
\Theta\colon\mathsf{C}(\mathsf{A})\longrightarrow\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{A})
has adjoint
functors on both sides,
G^{+}
and
G^{-}\colon\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{A})\longrightarrow\mathsf{C}(\mathsf{A})
,
which were constructed in Example
3.16
.

Let
\mathsf{E}
be an exact category.
Then the additive category
\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{E})
has a natural exact category
structure in which a pair of composable morphisms is an admissible
short exact sequence if and only if it is an admissible short exact
sequence in every degree.
Applying Lemma
4.13
to
the faithful, conservative forgetful functor

\Theta\colon\mathsf{C}(\mathsf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{E})
, one produces the (standard and
well-known) exact category structure on the category of
complexes
\mathsf{C}(\mathsf{E})
.
It is easy to check that the assumptions of the lemma hold: in fact,
the functor
\Theta
preserves kernels and cokernels, and any morphism
in
\mathsf{C}(\mathsf{E})
whose image under
\Theta
has a (co)kernel in

\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{E})
has a (co)kernel in
\mathsf{C}(\mathsf{E})
.

One can easily check that the exact category structure on

\mathsf{C}(\mathsf{E})=\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E}))
is DG-admissible.
Applying Theorem
4.17
, one produces
an exact DG-category structure on the DG-category
of complexes
\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E})
.

The composition
\Theta\circ G^{+}\colon\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{E})
of
the functors
G^{+}
and
\Theta
is isomorphic to the direct sum

\mathrm{Id}\oplus\mathrm{Id}[-1]
of the identity endofunctor and the shift functor.
Therefore, the functor
\Theta\circ G^{+}
preserves and reflects
admissible short exact sequences.
By construction, so does the functor
\Theta
.
It follows that the functor
G^{+}
also preserves and reflects
admissible short exact sequences.

Looking on the commutative diagram (
6
) and
keeping in mind that the functor
\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E})}
preserves and
reflects admissible short exact sequences by the definition of
an exact DG-category, one concludes that the functor
\Upsilon_{\mathsf{E}}

preserves and reflects admissible short exact sequences.
Thus the exact category structure on
\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{E})
is inherited from
the exact structure on
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E})^{\natural})
under the inclusion

\Upsilon_{\mathsf{E}}\colon\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{E})\rightarrowtail\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E})^{\natural})
.

The composition
\Phi_{\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E})}\circ\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E})}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E})^{\natural})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E})^{\natural})
of the functors

\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E})}
and
\Phi_{\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E})}
is also isomorphic to
the direct sum
\mathrm{Id}\oplus\mathrm{Id}[1]
of the identity endofunctor and
the shift functor.
Therefore, any short exact sequence in the exact category

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E})^{\natural})
is isomorphic to a direct summand of the image
of a short exact sequence from the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E}))

under the functor
\Phi_{\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E})}
.
In view of the commutative diagram (
5
),
it follows that any short exact sequence in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E})^{\natural})

is a direct summand of a short exact sequence in

\Upsilon_{\mathsf{E}}(\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{E}))
.

Let
\mathsf{F}\subset\mathsf{E}
be a full additive subcategory inheriting
an exact category structure.
Then the exact category structure on the category of graded objects

\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{F})
is inherited from the exact structure on
\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{E})
.
It follows that the exact structure on the category of complexes

\mathsf{C}(\mathsf{F})
is inherited from the exact structure on
\mathsf{C}(\mathsf{E})
.
Hence the exact DG-category structure on the DG-category of
complexes
\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{F})
is inherited from the exact DG-category
structure on
\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E})
.

Finally, let
\mathsf{A}
be an abelian category.
In this case, the functor
\Upsilon_{\mathsf{A}}\colon\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{A})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{A})^{\natural})
is an equivalence of categories (since
\mathsf{A}

is idempotent-complete; see Example
3.16
).
Both the category of graded objects
\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{A})
and the category
of complexes
\mathsf{C}(\mathsf{A})=\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{A}))
are abelian when
\mathsf{A}
is
abelian.
Thus the DG-category of complexes
\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{A})
is abelian.
The construction of the exact DG-category structure on the category
of complexes above produces the abelian exact DG-category structure
on
\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{A})
from the abelian exact category structure on
\mathsf{A}
.

Example 4.40
.

Let
\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(R^{*},d,h)
be a CDG-ring and
\mathbf{A}=\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}}
be
the DG-category of left CDG-modules over
\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.

Then
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
is the category of CDG-modules and closed
morphisms of degree
0
between them; one can easily see that it is
an abelian category.
In fact,
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
is equivalent to the abelian category

R^{*}[\delta]{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
of left graded modules over the graded ring

R^{*}[\delta]
from Section
3.1
(as explained
in the first paragraph of the proof of
Proposition
3.1
).

Furthermore, according to Example
3.17
,
the functor
\Upsilon_{\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}}\colon R^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})

is an equivalence between the category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})

and the abelian category of graded left
R^{*}
-modules.
So the category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
is abelian as well.

Thus the DG-category
\mathbf{A}=\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}}
of left CDG-modules
over a CDG-ring
\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is an abelian DG-category.

Example 4.41
.

Let
X
be a scheme and
\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B^{*},d,h)
be a quasi-coherent
CDG-quasi-algebra over
X
.
Let
\mathbf{A}=\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--qcoh}}}
be the DG-category of quasi-coherent left
CDG-modules over
\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
(as defined in
Section
2.4
).

Then
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
is the category of quasi-coherent CDG-modules
and closed morphisms between them.
Similarly to Example
4.40
,
this category is abelian (this fact was mentioned at the end of
Section
2.4
).
In fact,
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
is equivalent to the category of
quasi-coherent graded left modules over the quasi-coherent
graded quasi-algebra
B^{*}[\delta]
on
X
constructed in
Example
3.18
.

Furthermore, as explained in Example
3.18
,
the functor
\Upsilon_{\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}}\colon B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})

is an equivalence between the category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})

and the abelian category of quasi-coherent graded left
B^{*}
-modules.
Hence the category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
is abelian as well.

Therefore, the DG-category
\mathbf{A}=\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--qcoh}}}
of quasi-coherent
left CDG-modules over a quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebra
\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}

is an abelian DG-category.

Example 4.42
.

Let
\mathsf{A}
be an additive category and
\Lambda\colon\mathsf{A}\longrightarrow\mathsf{A}
be
an autoequivalence.
We continue the discussion of factorization categories started in
Section
2.5
and
Example
3.19
, and assume that either

\Gamma=\mathbb{Z}
, or
\Lambda
is involutive and
\Gamma=\mathbb{Z}/2
.
Let
w\colon\mathrm{Id}_{\mathsf{A}}\longrightarrow\Lambda^{2}
be a potential.

We consider the additive category
\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda)
of

\Lambda
-periodic objects in
\mathsf{A}
(and homogeneous morphisms
of degree
0
between them) and the additive category

\mathsf{F}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w)
of factorizations of
w
(and closed morphisms
of degree
0
between them).
Following Example
3.19
, the forgetful
functor
\Theta\colon\mathsf{F}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w)\longrightarrow\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda)
has
adjoint functors on both sides,
G^{+}
and
G^{-}\colon\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda)\longrightarrow\mathsf{F}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w)
.

Let
\mathsf{E}
be an exact category and
\Lambda\colon\mathsf{E}\longrightarrow\mathsf{E}
be
an autoequivalence preserving and reflecting admissible short exact
sequences.
Then the additive category of graded objects
\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{E})
has
the induced exact structure discussed in
Example
4.39
.
Applying Lemma
4.13

to the faithful, conservative forgetful functor
\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda)\longrightarrow\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{E})
, one obtains the induced exact category structure
on the category of
\Lambda
-periodic objects
\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda)

(in fact, the same exact structure can be simply obtained from
the category equivalence
\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda)\simeq\mathsf{E}
).
Applying the same lemma again to the faithful, conservative
forgetful functor
\Theta\colon\mathsf{F}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w)\longrightarrow\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda)
,
one produces an exact category structure on the additive category
of factorizations
\mathsf{F}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w)
.
In both cases, the forgetful functor preserves kernels and cokernels,
and any morphism in the source category whose image in the target
category has a (co)kernel has a (co)kernel in the source category;
so the assumptions of lemma hold.

One can easily check that the resulting exact category structure on

\mathsf{F}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w)=\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w))
is DG-admissible.
In fact, let
\mathbf{E}
denote the DG-category of factorizations

\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w)
.
Then one observes that, for any object
\boldsymbol{X}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\in\mathbf{E}
, the forgetful
functor
\Theta\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda)
takes
the canonical sequence
0\longrightarrow\boldsymbol{X}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}[-1]\longrightarrow\Xi_{\mathbf{E}}(\boldsymbol{X}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})\longrightarrow\boldsymbol{X}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow 0
to a split short exact sequence in

\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda)
.
Applying Theorem
4.17
, one produces
an exact DG-category structure on the DG-category
of factorizations
\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w)
.

Similarly to Example
4.39
,
the composition
\Theta\circ G^{+}\colon\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda)\longrightarrow\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda)
of the functors
G^{+}
and
\Theta
is isomorphic
to the direct sum
\mathrm{Id}\oplus\mathrm{Id}[-1]
of the identity endofunctor
and the shift functor.
(Basically, the reason for this splitting behavior is that
factorizations are similar to CDG-modules over a CDG-ring with
zero differential; cf. the formula for the action of
R^{*}

in
G^{+}(M^{*})
in Example
3.17
.)
It follows that the functor
\Theta\circ G^{+}
preserves and
reflects admissible short exact sequences, and one can conclude
that so does the functor
G^{+}
.

Looking on the commutative diagram (
14
)
and remembering that the functor
\Psi_{\mathbf{E}}^{+}
preserves and reflects
admissible short exact sequences by the definition of an exact
DG-category, one concludes that the functor
\Upsilon_{\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w}

preserves and reflects admissible short exact sequences.
Thus the exact category structure on
\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda)
is inherited
from the exact structure on
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
under the inclusion

\Upsilon_{\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w}\colon\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda)\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w)^{\natural})
.

The composition
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}\circ\Psi_{\mathbf{E}}^{+}\colon\mathsf{Z}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
of the functors
\Psi_{\mathbf{E}}^{+}
and
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}
is
also isomorphic to the direct sum
\mathrm{Id}\oplus\mathrm{Id}[1]
of the identity
endofunctor and the shift functor.
Therefore, any short exact sequence in the exact category

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
is isomorphic to a direct summand of the image
of a short exact sequence from the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})

under the functor
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}
.
In view of the commutative
diagram (
13
), it follows that
any short exact sequence in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w)^{\natural})
is
a direct summand of a short exact sequence in

\Upsilon_{\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w}(\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda))
.

Let
\mathsf{H}\subset\mathsf{E}
be a full additive subcategory preserved by
the autoequivalences
\Lambda
and
\Lambda^{-1}
and inheriting
an exact category structure from
\mathsf{E}
.
(We will denote the restrictions of
\Lambda
and
w
onto
\mathsf{H}

simply by
\Lambda
and
w
.)
Then the exact category structure on the category of

\Lambda
-periodic objects
\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{H},\Lambda)
is inherited from
the exact structure on
\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda)
.
It follows that the exact structure on the category of factorizations

\mathsf{F}(\mathsf{H},\Lambda,w)
is inherited from the exact structure on

\mathsf{F}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w)
.
Therefore, the exact DG-category structure on the DG-category of
factorizations
\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{H},\Lambda,w)
is inherited from the exact
DG-category structure on
\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w)
.

Finally, assume that
\mathsf{A}
is an abelian category.
Then the functor
\Upsilon_{\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w}\colon\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda)\longrightarrow\mathsf{F}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w)^{\natural})
is an equivalence of categories (since
\mathsf{A}

is idempotent-complete; see Example
3.19
).
Both the category of
\Lambda
-periodic objects
\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda)

and the category of factorizations
\mathsf{F}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w)=\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w))
are abelian.
Thus the DG-category of factorizations
\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w)
is
abelian.
The construction of the exact DG-category structure on the category
of factorizations above produces the abelian exact DG-category
structure on
\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w)
from the abelian exact category
structure on
\mathsf{A}
.

5.
Derived Categories of the Second Kind

5.1.
Definitions of derived categories of the second kind

The idea of defining derived categories of the second kind for exact
DG-categories was suggested in
[
35
, Remarks 3.5–3.7]
.
This section provides the implementation.

Let
\mathbf{E}
be an exact DG-category (see the definition in
Section
4.3
).
In particular,
\mathbf{E}
is an additive DG-category with shifts and
cones, so its homotopy category
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
is triangulated.
We refer to Section
1.3

and
[
35
, Section 1.2]
for the definitions of totalizations
of complexes in DG-categories.

Any short exact sequence in the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
can be
viewed as a three-term complex in the DG-category
\mathbf{E}
.
Denote by
{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})
the minimal thick subcategory in
the homotopy category
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
containing the totalizations of
(admissible) short exact sequences in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
.
The objects of
{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})
are said to be
absolutely
acyclic
(with respect to the given exact DG-category structure
on
\mathbf{E}
).
The triangulated Verdier quotient category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})=\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})/{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})

is called the
absolute derived category
of an exact
DG-category
\mathbf{E}
.

Assume that infinite coproducts exist in a DG-category
\mathbf{E}
.
We recall that in this case such coproducts also exist in
the DG-category
\mathbf{E}^{\natural}
and in the additive categories

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
and
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
, as well as in the triangulated
category
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
.
Moreover, the coproducts are preserved by the additive functors

\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
and

\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{E}}
,
\Psi^{-}_{\mathbf{E}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
,
since all of them are left (as well as right) adjoint functors.

Dually, assume that infinite products exist in a DG-category
\mathbf{E}
.
Then such products also exist in the DG-category
\mathbf{E}^{\natural}
and
in the additive categories
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
and
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
, as well
as in the triangulated category
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
.
Moreover, the products are preserved by the additive functors

\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
and

\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{E}}
,
\Psi^{-}_{\mathbf{E}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
.

Let
\mathsf{E}
be an exact category with infinite coproducts.
Then we will say that
the coproducts are exact
in
\mathsf{E}
if
the coproduct of any family of (admissible) short exact sequences
is a short exact sequence in
\mathsf{E}
.
Dually, if
\mathsf{E}
is an exact category with infinite products,
then we will say that
the products are exact
in
\mathsf{E}
whenever
the product of an arbitrary family of short exact sequences is
a short exact sequence.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
\mathbf{E}
be an exact DG-category.

(a)
Assume that infinite coproducts exist in
the DG-category
\mathbf{E}
.
Then the coproducts are exact in the exact category
\mathsf{Z}(\mathbf{E})
if
and only if they are exact in the exact category
\mathsf{Z}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.

(b)
Assume that infinite products exist in
the DG-category
\mathbf{E}
.
Then the products are exact in the exact category
\mathsf{Z}(\mathbf{E})
if
and only if they are exact in the exact category
\mathsf{Z}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.

Proof.

By the definition of an exact DG-category, short exact sequences
are preserved and reflected by the additive functors
\Phi

and
\Psi^{\pm}
.
Since the products and coproducts are also preserved by these
functors, the assertions follow.
∎

We will say that an exact DG-category
\mathbf{E}

has exact
coproducts
if infinite coproducts exist in the DG-category
\mathbf{E}

and the equivalent conditions of
Lemma
5.1
(a) hold.
Dually, an exact DG-category
\mathbf{E}
is said to
have exact
products
if infinite products exist in the DG-category
\mathbf{E}

and the equivalent conditions of
Lemma
5.1
(b) hold.

Let
\mathbf{E}
be an exact DG-category with exact coproducts.
Denote by
{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{E})
the minimal triangulated subcategory in
the homotopy category
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
containing the totalizations
of short exact sequences in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
and closed under
coproducts in
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
.
The objects of
{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{E})
are called
coacyclic

(with respect to the given exact DG-category structure on
\mathbf{E}
).
The triangulated Verdier quotient category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{E})=\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})/{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{E})

is called the
coderived category
of an exact DG-category
\mathbf{E}
.

Dually, let
\mathbf{E}
be an exact DG-category with exact products.
Denote by
{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{E})
the minimal triangulated subcategory in
the homotopy category
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
containing the totalizations of
short exact sequences in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
and closed under products
in
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
.
The objects of
{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{E})
are called
contraacyclic

(with respect to the given exact DG-category structure on
\mathbf{E}
).
The triangulated quotient category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{E})=\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})/{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{E})

is called the
contraderived category
of an exact
DG-category
\mathbf{E}
.

Notice that any triangulated category with countable (co)products
is idempotent-complete
[
11
, Proposition 3.2]
.
Consequently, any triangulated subcategory closed under countable
(co)products in a triangulated category with countable (co)products
is a thick subcategory
[
29
, Criterion 1.3]
.
Hence all absolutely acyclic objects are coacyclic and contraacyclic
(under the respective assumptions).

5.2.
Injective and projective resolutions: semiorthogonality

Let
\mathsf{E}
be an exact category.
An object
P\in\mathsf{E}
is said to be
projective
if the functor

\Hom_{\mathsf{E}}(P,{-})\colon\allowbreak\mathsf{E}\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}{\operatorname{\mathrm{--mod}}}
is exact, i. e.,
it takes (admissible) short exact sequences in
\mathsf{E}
to short exact
sequences of abelian groups.
Dually, an object
J\in\mathsf{E}
is said to be
injective
if
the functor
\Hom_{\mathsf{E}}({-},J)\colon\mathsf{E}^{\mathsf{op}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}{\operatorname{\mathrm{--mod}}}
takes short
exact sequences in
\mathsf{E}
to short exact sequences of abelian groups.
We will denote the full subcategory of projective objects by

\mathsf{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}\subset\mathsf{E}
and the full subcategory of injective objects
by
\mathsf{E}_{\mathsf{inj}}\subset\mathsf{E}
.

One says that an exact category
\mathsf{E}
has
enough projectives

if every object of
\mathsf{E}
is the target of an admissible epimorphism
from a projective object.
Dually,
\mathsf{E}
has
enough injectives
if every object of
\mathsf{E}

is the source of an admissible monomorphism into an injective
object
[
14
, Section 11]
.

Remark 5.2
.

In any exact category with infinite products and enough projectives,
the product functors are exact.
Dually, in any exact category with infinite coproducts and enough
injectives, the coproduct functors are exact.

Indeed, let
0\longrightarrow X_{\alpha}\longrightarrow Y_{\alpha}\longrightarrow Z_{\alpha}\longrightarrow 0
be a family of short exact sequences in an exact
category
\mathsf{E}
.
Assume that the products
\prod_{\alpha}X_{\alpha}
,
\prod_{\alpha}Y_{\alpha}
, and
\prod_{\alpha}Z_{\alpha}
exist in
\mathsf{E}
, and consider
the sequence
0\longrightarrow\prod_{\alpha}X_{\alpha}\longrightarrow\prod_{\alpha}Y_{\alpha}\longrightarrow\prod_{\alpha}Z_{\alpha}\longrightarrow 0
.
Since infinite products always preserve kernels, the morphism

\prod_{\alpha}X_{\alpha}\longrightarrow\prod_{\alpha}Y_{\alpha}
is a kernel
of the morphism
\prod_{\alpha}Y_{\alpha}\longrightarrow\prod_{\alpha}Z_{\alpha}
.

Let
P
be a projective object in
\mathsf{E}
such that there is
an admissible epimorphism
P\longrightarrow\prod_{\alpha}Z_{\alpha}
.
Then we have a family of morphisms
P\longrightarrow Z_{\alpha}
, each of
which can be lifted to a morphism
P\longrightarrow Y_{\alpha}
.
Consequently, there is a morphism
P\longrightarrow\prod_{\alpha}Y_{\alpha}

making the triangle diagram
P\longrightarrow\prod_{\alpha}Y_{\alpha}\longrightarrow\prod_{\alpha}Z_{\alpha}
commutative.
By the “obscure axiom” (the dual assertion
to
[
14
, Proposition 2.16]
), it follows that the morphism

\prod_{\alpha}Y_{\alpha}\longrightarrow\prod_{\alpha}Z_{\alpha}
is
an admissible epimorphism.
Thus the short sequence of products
0\longrightarrow\prod_{\alpha}X_{\alpha}\longrightarrow\prod_{\alpha}Y_{\alpha}\longrightarrow\prod_{\alpha}Z_{\alpha}\longrightarrow 0

is exact in
\mathsf{E}
.

Let
\mathbf{E}
be an exact DG-category.
An object
P\in\mathbf{E}
is said to be
graded-projective
if

\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(P)\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
is a projective object of the exact
category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.
Dually, an object
J\in\mathbf{E}
is said to be
graded-injective

if
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(J)\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
is an injective object of
the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.
We will denote the full DG-subcategory of graded-projective
objects by
\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{proj}}\subset\mathbf{E}
and the full DG-subcategory of
graded-injective objects by
\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{inj}}\subset\mathbf{E}
.
The following examples explain the terminology.

Examples 5.3
.

(1) Let
\mathsf{A}
be an exact category.
Consider the exact DG-category structure on the DG-category of
complexes
\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{A})
constructed in
Example
4.39
.

According to diagram (
5
),
the forgetful functor
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{A}))\longrightarrow\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{A})
forms
a commutative diagram with the functor
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{A}))\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{A})^{\natural})
and the fully faithful functor

\Upsilon_{\mathsf{A}}\colon\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{A})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{A})^{\natural})
.
Moreover, according to the discussion in
Example
4.39
, the exact category
structure on
\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{A})
is inherited from the exact category
structure on
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{A})^{\natural})
via the embedding
\Upsilon_{\mathsf{A}}
,
and any short exact sequence in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{A})^{\natural})
is a direct
summand of a short exact sequence coming from
\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{A})
.

Consequently, an object of
\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{A})
is graded-projective
(respectively, graded-injective) if and only if its image under
the forgetful functor
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{A}))\longrightarrow\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{A})
is
projective (resp., injective).
In other words, the graded-projective objects in
\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{A})

are the complexes of projective objects in
\mathsf{A}
and
the graded-injective objects in
\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{A})
are the complexes of
injective objects in
\mathsf{A}
, that is
\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{A})_{\mathbf{proj}}=\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{A}_{\mathsf{proj}})

and
\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{A})_{\mathbf{inj}}=\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{A}_{\mathsf{inj}})
.

(2) Let
\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(R^{*},d,h)
be a CDG-ring and
\mathbf{A}=\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}}

be the DG-category of left CDG-modules over
\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
We endow
\mathbf{A}
with the abelian exact DG-category structure,
as per Example
4.40
.

The functor
\Upsilon_{\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}}\colon R^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}})
establishes an equivalence between the abelian
category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}})
and the abelian category of graded
left
R^{*}
-modules.
According to diagram (
8
),
the forgetful functor
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}})\longrightarrow R^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}

forms a commutative diagram with the functor

\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}((\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}})^{\natural})

and the category equivalence
\Upsilon_{\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}}
.

Consequently, a CDG-module over
\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is graded-projective
(resp., graded-injective) in the sense of the definition above
if and only if its underlying graded
R^{*}
-module is projective
(resp., injective) as an object of the abelian category of
graded
R^{*}
-modules.

(3) Let
X
be a scheme and
\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B^{*},d,h)
be a quasi-coherent
CDG-quasi-algebra over
X
.
Let
\mathbf{A}=\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--qcoh}}}
be the DG-category of quasi-coherent left
CDG-modules over
\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
We endow
\mathbf{A}
with the abelian exact DG-category structure,
as per Example
4.41
.

The functor
\Upsilon_{\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}}\colon B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--qcoh}}})
establishes an equivalence between the abelian
category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--qcoh}}})
and the abelian category of
quasi-coherent left
B^{*}
-modules.
According to diagram (
11
),
the forgetful functor
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--qcoh}}})\longrightarrow B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--qcoh}}}

forms a commutative diagram with the functor

\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--qcoh}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}((\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--qcoh}}})^{\natural})

and the category equivalence
\Upsilon_{\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}}
.

Consequently, a quasi-coherent CDG-module over
\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is
graded-injective in the sense of the definition above if and only if
its underlying quasi-coherent graded
B^{*}
-module is injective
as an object of the abelian category of quasi-coherent graded

B^{*}
-modules.

(4) Let
\mathsf{A}
be an exact category and
\Lambda\colon\mathsf{A}\longrightarrow\mathsf{A}
be
its autoequivalence preserving and reflecting admissible short
exact sequences.
We assume that either
\Gamma=\mathbb{Z}
, or
\Lambda
is involutive
and
\Gamma=\mathbb{Z}/2
.
Let
w\colon\mathrm{Id}_{\mathsf{A}}\colon\mathrm{Id}_{\mathsf{A}}\longrightarrow\Lambda^{2}
be a potential.
Consider the exact DG-category structure on the DG-category of
factorizations
\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w)
constructed in
Example
4.42
.

According to diagram (
13
),
the forgetful functor
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda)
forms
a commutative diagram with the functor
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
and the fully faithful functor

\Upsilon_{\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w}\colon\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda)\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.
Moreover, according to the discussion in
Example
4.42
, the exact category
structure on
\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda)
is inherited from the exact category
structure on
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
via the embedding

\Upsilon_{\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w}
, and any short exact sequence in

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
is a direct summand of a short exact sequence
coming from
\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda)
.

Consequently, an object of
\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w)
is graded-projective
(respectively, graded-injective) if and only if its image under
the forgetful functor
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w))\longrightarrow\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda)
is projective (resp., injective).
In other words, the graded-projective objects in
\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w)

are the factorizations with projective components in
\mathsf{A}
and
the graded-injective objects in
\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w)
are
the factorizations with injective components in
\mathsf{A}
, that is

\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w)_{\mathbf{proj}}=\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{A}_{\mathsf{proj}},\Lambda,w)
and

\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w)_{\mathbf{inj}}=\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{A}_{\mathsf{inj}},\Lambda,w)
.
(Here the restrictions of
\Lambda
and
w
to
\mathsf{A}_{\mathsf{proj}}
and

\mathsf{A}_{\mathsf{inj}}
are denoted simply by
\Lambda
and
w
for brevity.)

The reader can find a detailed discussion of projective and injective
objects in abelian DG-categories in
[
53
, Sections 6.1 and 7.1]
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
\mathbf{E}
be an exact DG-category.

(a)
The full DG-subcategory of graded-projective objects

\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{proj}}\subset\mathbf{E}
is additive, and closed under shifts and twists
(hence also under cones) and direct summands.
The full DG-subcategory
\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{proj}}\subset\mathbf{E}
inherits an exact
DG-category structure.
The inclusion
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{proj}}\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}((\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{proj}})^{\natural})

holds in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.
If
\mathbf{E}
has infinite coproducts, then
\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{proj}}
is closed
under infinite coproducts in
\mathbf{E}
.

(b)
The full DG-subcategory of graded-injective objects

\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{inj}}\subset\mathbf{E}
is additive, and closed under shifts and twists
(hence also under cones) and direct summands.
The full DG-subcategory
\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{inj}}\subset\mathbf{E}
inherits an exact
DG-category structure.
The inclusion
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{inj}}\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}((\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{inj}})^{\natural})

holds in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.
If
\mathbf{E}
has infinite products, then
\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{inj}}
is closed under
infinite products in
\mathbf{E}
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove part (a), as part (b) is dual.
Let
\mathsf{L}=\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{proj}}
be the full subcategory of projective
objects in the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.
The full subcategory
\mathsf{L}
is additive, closed under extensions and
direct summands, and it is preserved by the shift functors acting
naturally on
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.
Hence Proposition
4.27
(a–d) is applicable,
implying the first three assertions of part (a).
The full DG-subcategory
\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{proj}}\subset\mathbf{E}
is closed under
infinite coproducts because the full subcategory of projective objects

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{proj}}\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
is closed under
coproducts and the functor
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}
preserves coproducts.
∎

Notice that the inclusions
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{proj}}\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}((\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{proj}})^{\natural})
and
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{inj}}\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}((\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{inj}})^{\natural})
in Lemma
5.4

can be strict, as Example
4.28
illustrates.

Theorem 5.5
.

(a)
Let
\mathbf{E}
be an exact DG-category with exact coproducts.
Then for any objects
J\in\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{inj}})
and
X\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{E})

one has
\Hom_{\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})}(X,J)=0
.
Consequently, the composition of the triangulated inclusion functor

\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{inj}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
and the Verdier quotient functor

\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{E})
is a fully faithful triangulated
functor
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{inj}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{E})
.

(b)
Let
\mathbf{E}
be an exact DG-category with exact products.
Then for any objects
P\in\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{proj}})
and
Y\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{E})

one has
\Hom_{\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})}(P,Y)=0
.
Consequently, the composition of the triangulated inclusion functor

\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{proj}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
and the Verdier quotient functor

\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{E})
is a fully faithful triangulated
functor
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{proj}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{E})
.

Proof.

This is a generalization of
[
35
, Theorem in Section 3.5]

suggested in
[
35
, Remark in Section 3.5]
.
Let us prove part (b) (part (a) is dual).
The second assertion in (b) follows from the first one in view of
the well-known general properties of semiorthogonal triangulated
subcategories.
Furthermore, for any object
P\in\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
the class of all
objects
Y\in\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
such that
\Hom_{\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})}(P,Y[*])=0
is
a full triangulated subcategory closed under infinite products
in
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
.

It remains to consider a short exact sequence
0\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow B\longrightarrow C\longrightarrow 0
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
and its totalization
T\in\mathbf{E}
.
Let
P\in\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{proj}})
be a graded-projective object.
In order to show that the complex of abelian groups

\Hom^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\mathbf{E}}(P,T)
is acyclic, we observe that, by the definition
of the totalization,
\Hom^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\mathbf{E}}(P,T)
is the totalization of
the bicomplex with three rows
\Hom^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\mathbf{E}}(P,A)\longrightarrow\Hom^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\mathbf{E}}(P,B)\longrightarrow\Hom^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\mathbf{E}}(P,C)
.
Clearly, the totalization of any finite acyclic complex of complexes
of abelian groups is acyclic; so it suffices to check that

0\longrightarrow\Hom^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\mathbf{E}}(P,A)\longrightarrow\Hom^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\mathbf{E}}(P,B)\longrightarrow\Hom^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\mathbf{E}}(P,C)\longrightarrow 0
is a short exact sequence of complexes
of abelian groups.
For this purpose, we only need to show that
0\longrightarrow\Hom^{0}_{\mathbf{E}}(P,A)\longrightarrow\Hom^{0}_{\mathbf{E}}(P,B)\longrightarrow\Hom^{0}_{\mathbf{E}}(P,C)\longrightarrow 0

is a short exact sequence of abelian groups (as all the classes of
objects involved are obviously closed under shifts in
\mathbf{E}
).

Now we recall that the additive functor
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
can be naturally extended to a fully faithful
additive functor
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\mathbf{E}}\colon\mathbf{E}^{0}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
,
by Lemma
3.9
.
So the desired assertion can be rephrased by saying that the short
sequence of abelian groups
0\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})}(\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(P),\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(A))\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})}(\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(P),\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(B))\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})}(\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(P),\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(C))\longrightarrow 0
is
exact.
Finally, it remains to use the assumption that
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(P)
is
a projective object in the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
, together
with the fact that the short sequence
0\longrightarrow\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(A)\longrightarrow\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(B)\longrightarrow\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(C)\longrightarrow 0
is exact in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})

(as the functor
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
is
exact by the definition of an exact DG-category).
∎

5.3.
Injective and projective resolutions: finite homological
dimension case

Let
\mathsf{E}
be an exact category.
Given two objects
X
and
Y\in\mathsf{E}
, the Ext groups

\Ext_{\mathsf{E}}^{n}(X,Y)
,
n\geq 0
, can be defined using the Yoneda
Ext construction.
Equivalently, put
\Ext_{\mathsf{E}}^{n}(X,Y)=\Hom_{\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{E})}(X,Y[n])
,
where
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{E})
denotes the bounded derived category of
the exact category
\mathsf{E}
(see, e. g.,
[
29
]

and
[
36
, Section A.7]
).
We refer to Section
8.3
below
for a further discussion.

An object
A\in\mathsf{E}
is said to have
injective
dimension
\leq n
if
\Ext_{\mathsf{E}}^{i}(X,A)=0
for all
X\in\mathsf{E}
and
i>n
.
If this is the case, we write
\idi_{\mathsf{E}}A\leq n
.
Dually, an object
B\in\mathsf{E}
is said to have
projective
dimension
\leq n
if
\Ext_{\mathsf{E}}^{i}(B,Y)=0
for all
Y\in\mathsf{E}
and
i>n
.
If this is the case, we write
\pdi_{\mathsf{E}}B\leq n
.
An exact category
\mathsf{E}
is said to have
homological
dimension
\leq n
if
\Ext_{\mathsf{E}}^{i}(X,Y)=0
for all
X
,
Y\in\mathsf{E}

and
i>n
.

Assume that the exact category
\mathsf{E}
has enough projective objects.
Then an object
B\in\mathsf{E}
has projective dimension
\leq n
if and
only if it admits a projective resolution
0\longrightarrow P_{n}\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow P_{0}\longrightarrow B\longrightarrow 0
of length
n
.
In fact, if
\pdi_{\mathsf{E}}B\leq n
and
0\longrightarrow Q\longrightarrow P_{n-1}\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow P_{0}\longrightarrow B\longrightarrow 0
is an exact complex with
projective objects
P_{i}\in\mathsf{E}
, then
Q\in\mathsf{E}
is also a projective
object.

Dually, assume that
\mathsf{E}
has enough injective objects.
Then an object
A\in\mathsf{E}
has injective dimension
\leq n
if and
only if it admits an injective coresolution
0\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow J^{0}\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow J^{n}\longrightarrow 0
of length
n
.
In fact, if
\idi_{\mathsf{E}}A\leq n
and
0\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow J^{0}\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow J^{n-1}\longrightarrow K\longrightarrow 0
is an exact complex with
injective objects
J^{i}\in\mathsf{E}
, then
K\in\mathsf{E}
is also an injective
object.

Theorem 5.6
.

(a)
Let
\mathbf{E}
be an exact DG-category with infinite
coproducts.
Assume that the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
has finite
homological dimension and enough injective objects.
Then the two thick subcategories
{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})
and

{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{E})
in the homotopy category
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
coincide.
Furthermore, the composition of the triangulated inclusion functor

\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{inj}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
and the Verdier quotient functor

\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{E})
is a triangulated equivalence.
So one has
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{E})\simeq\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{inj}})
.

(b)
Let
\mathbf{E}
be an exact DG-category with infinite
products.
Assume that the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
has finite
homological dimension and enough projective objects.
Then the two thick subcategories
{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})
and

{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{E})
in the homotopy category
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
coincide.
Furthermore, the composition of the triangulated inclusion functor

\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{proj}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
and the Verdier quotient functor

\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{E})
is a triangulated equivalence.
So one has
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{E})\simeq\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{proj}})
.

Proof.

This is the generalization of
[
35
, Theorem in Section 3.6]

suggested in
[
35
, Remark in Section 3.6]
.
We start with several lemmas.

Lemma 5.7
.

(a)
Let
\mathbf{E}
be an exact DG-category,
A\in\mathbf{E}
and

K\in\mathbf{E}^{\natural}
be two objects, and
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(A)\longrightarrow K
be
an admissible monomorphism in the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.
Then the corresponding morphism
A\longrightarrow\Psi^{-}_{\mathbf{E}}(K)

(by adjunction) is an admissible monomorphism in the exact category

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
.

(b)
Let
\mathbf{E}
be an exact DG-category,
B\in\mathbf{E}
and

L\in\mathbf{E}^{\natural}
be two objects, and
L\longrightarrow\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(B)
be
an admissible epimorphism in the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.
Then the corresponding morphism
\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{E}}(L)\longrightarrow B

(by adjunction) is an admissible epimorphism in the exact category

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
.

Proof.

Let us prove part (b) (part (a) is dual).
The functor
\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{E}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
is exact
by the definition of an exact DG-category, so it takes admissible
epimorphisms to admissible epimorphisms.
Hence
\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{E}}(L)\longrightarrow\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{E}}\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(B)
is an admissible
epimorphism.
On the other hand, by Lemma
3.8
and by the definition
of a DG-compatible exact structure, we have a natural short exact
sequence
0\longrightarrow B[-1]\longrightarrow\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{E}}\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(B)\longrightarrow B\longrightarrow 0

in the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
.
In particular, the adjunction morphism
\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{E}}\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(B)\longrightarrow B

is an admissible epimorphism.
Thus the composition
\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{E}}(L)\longrightarrow\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{E}}\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(B)\longrightarrow B
is an admissible epimorphism.
∎

Lemma 5.8
.

(a)
Let
\mathbf{E}
be an exact DG-category for which
the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
has enough injective objects.
Then for any object
A\in\mathbf{E}
there exists an admissible monomorphism

A\longrightarrow J
in the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
such that the object

\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(J)
is injective in the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.

(b)
Let
\mathbf{E}
be an exact DG-category for which
the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
has enough projective objects.
Then for any object
B\in\mathbf{E}
there exists an admissible epimorphism

P\longrightarrow B
in the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
such that the object

\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(P)
is projective in the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.

Proof.

Let us prove part (b) (part (a) is dual).
Given an object
B\in\mathbf{E}
, consider the object
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(B)\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
and choose a projective object

Q\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{proj}}
together with an admissible epimorphism

Q\longrightarrow\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(B)
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.
By Lemma
5.7
(b), the related morphism

\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{E}}(Q)\longrightarrow B
is an admissible epimorphism in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
.

By Lemma
3.8
and by the definition of
a DG-compatible exact structure, we have a natural short exact
sequence
0\longrightarrow Q\longrightarrow\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{E}}(Q)\longrightarrow Q[1]\longrightarrow 0

in the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.
Hence the object
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{E}}(Q)\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
is
projective as an extension of two projective objects, and we can put

P=\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{E}}(Q)
.
∎

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
\mathbf{E}
be an exact DG-category, and let

0\longrightarrow X^{0}\longrightarrow X^{1}\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow X^{n}\longrightarrow 0

be a finite exact complex in the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
.
Then the totalization
\Tot(X^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is an absolutely acyclic
object of
\mathbf{E}
.

Proof.

Let
0\longrightarrow K^{i}\longrightarrow X^{i}\longrightarrow K^{i+1}\longrightarrow 0
be the short
exact sequences in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
from which the exact complex
X^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

is obtained by splicing.
Then the totalization
\Tot(X^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is homotopy equivalent to
an object obtainable from the totalizations

\Tot(K^{i}\to X^{i}\to K^{i+1})
as an iterated cone of natural
closed morphisms.
∎

For a converse assertion to
Lemma
5.9
,
see Proposition
8.2
below.

Let us prove part (b) of the theorem (part (a) is dual).
Our aim is to show that any object
B\in\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
can be included
into a distinguished triangle
P\longrightarrow B\longrightarrow Y\longrightarrow P[1]

in
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
with
P\in\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{proj}})
and
Y\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})
.
Then all the assertions of (b) follow in view of
Theorem
5.5
(b).
In this argument, we use tht inclusion
{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})\subset{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{E})
.
Notice that the infinite produts are exact in
\mathbf{E}
by
Remark
5.2
;
so Theorem
5.5
(b)
is applicable.

Let
B\in\mathbf{E}
be an object.
By Lemma
5.8
(b), there is
a short exact sequence
0\longrightarrow B_{1}\longrightarrow P_{0}\longrightarrow B\longrightarrow 0

in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
with
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(P_{0})\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{proj}}
.
Applying the same lemma to the object
B_{1}\in\mathbf{E}
, we obtain
a short exact sequence
0\longrightarrow B_{2}\longrightarrow P_{1}\longrightarrow B_{1}\longrightarrow 0

in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
with
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(P_{1})\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{proj}}
, etc.
Proceeding in this way, we construct an exact complex

0\longrightarrow Q\longrightarrow P_{n-1}\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow P_{0}\longrightarrow B\longrightarrow 0

in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
with
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(P_{i})\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{proj}}
,
where
n
is the homological dimension of the exact category

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.
Then the complex
0\longrightarrow\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(Q)\longrightarrow\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(P_{n-1})\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(P_{0})\longrightarrow\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(B)\longrightarrow 0
is
exact in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
, and it follows that

\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(Q)\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{proj}}
.

Put
P_{n}=Q
, and denote by
P
the totalization of the finite complex

P_{n}\longrightarrow P_{n-1}\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow P_{0}
in
the DG-category
\mathbf{E}
.
By Lemma
3.11
, the functor

\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
transforms twists into
isomorphisms, and it follows that the object
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(P)
is
isomorphic to the direct sum
\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(P_{i})[-i]
in
the additive category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.
Hence the object
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(P)
is projective in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.
Finally, the cone
Y
of the natural closed morphism
P\longrightarrow B

in
\mathbf{E}
is the totalization of the finite exact complex

0\longrightarrow P_{n}\longrightarrow P_{n-1}\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow P_{1}\longrightarrow P_{0}\longrightarrow B\longrightarrow 0
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
; so it is absolutely acyclic by
Lemma
5.9
.
∎

One can notice that the triangulated equivalences

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})\simeq\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{inj}})
in
Theorem
5.6
(a)
and
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})\simeq\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{proj}})

in Theorem
5.6
(b)
do not depend on the assumption of existence of (co)products in
\mathbf{E}
,
as it is clear from the proofs of
Theorems
5.5

and
5.6
above.

For a far-reaching generalization of the first assertions of
both parts (a) and (b) of
Theorem
5.6
,
see Theorem
8.9
below.
For a generalization of the second assertions of both parts of
Theorem
5.6
, see
Theorem
5.10
.

5.4.
Injective and projective resolutions: semiorthogonal
decompositions

The following two technical conditions on an exact category (going
back to
[
35
, Sections 3.7 and 3.8]
) will appear in
the main result of this section.

Let
\mathsf{E}
be an exact category with infinite coproducts and enough
injective objects.
We will say that
\mathsf{E}
satisfies (
*
) if

(
*
)

Any countable coproduct of injective objects in
\mathsf{E}

has finite injective dimension.

One can easily see that (
*
) implies existence of a finite integer

n\geq 0
such that any countable coproduct of injective objects
in
\mathsf{E}
has injective dimension
\leq n
.

Dually, let
\mathsf{E}
be an exact category with infinite products and
enough projective objects.
We will say that
\mathsf{E}
satisfies (
**
) if

(
**
)

Any countable product of projective objects in
\mathsf{E}

has finite projective dimension.

Existence of a finite integer
n\geq 0
such that any countable
product of projective objects in
\mathsf{E}
has projective dimension
\leq n

follows easily from (
**
).

Theorem 5.10
.

(a)
Let
\mathbf{E}
be an exact DG-category with twists
and infinite coproducts.
Assume that the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
has enough
injective objects and satisfies
(
*
)
.
Then the composition of the triangulated inclusion functor

\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{inj}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
and the Verdier quotient functor

\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{E})
is a triangulated equivalence

\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{inj}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{E})
.

(b)
Let
\mathbf{E}
be an exact DG-category with twists
and infinite products.
Assume that the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
has enough
projective objects and satisfies
(
**
)
.
Then the composition of the triangulated inclusion functor

\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{proj}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
and the Verdier quotient functor

\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{E})
is a triangulated equivalence

\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{proj}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{E})
.

For a far-reaching generalization of
Theorem
5.10
, see
Theorem
8.16
below.

Proof.

Notice that existence of infinite coproducts in
\mathbf{E}
and enough
injective objects in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
implies exactness of
coproducts in
\mathbf{E}
by Lemma
5.1

and Remark
5.2
.
Dually, existence of infinite products in
\mathbf{E}
and enough
projective objects in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
implies exactness of
products in
\mathbf{E}
.

The two dual parts of the theorem are the generalizations
of
[
35
, Theorems in Sections 3.7 and 3.8]
hinted at
in
[
35
, Remark in Section 3.7]
.
We start with a lemma generalizing
[
34
, Lemmas 2.1 and 4.1]
.

Lemma 5.11
.

(a)
Let
\mathbf{E}
be an exact DG-category with twists and
exact coproducts, and let
0\longrightarrow X^{0}\longrightarrow X^{1}\longrightarrow X^{2}\longrightarrow\dotsb
be a bounded below exact complex in the exact category

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
.
Then the coproduct totalization
\Tot^{\sqcup}(X^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is
a coacyclic object of
\mathbf{E}
.

(b)
Let
\mathbf{E}
be an exact DG-category with twists and
exact products, and let
\dotsb\longrightarrow Y_{2}\longrightarrow Y_{1}\longrightarrow Y_{0}\longrightarrow 0
be a bounded above exact complex in the exact category

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
.
Then the product totalization
\Tot^{\sqcap}(Y_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is
a contraacyclic object of
\mathbf{E}
.

Proof.

Let us prove part (b) (part (a) is dual).
Let
0\longrightarrow K_{i}\longrightarrow Y_{i}\longrightarrow K_{i-1}\longrightarrow 0
be the short
exact sequences in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
from which the exact complex
Y_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

is obtained by splicing.
Then the finite complex
0\longrightarrow K_{n}\longrightarrow Y_{n}\longrightarrow Y_{n-1}\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow Y_{1}\longrightarrow Y_{0}\longrightarrow 0
is exact in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
for
every
n\geq 1
.
By Lemma
5.9
,
the objects
L_{n}=\Tot(K_{n}\to Y_{n}\to\dotsb\to Y_{1}\to Y_{0})\in\mathbf{E}

are absolutely acyclic.

Consider the telescope sequence

(23)

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Tot^{\sqcap}(Y_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\prod\nolimits_{n\geq 1}L_{n}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\prod\nolimits_{n\geq 1}L_{n}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0.

In view of Lemma
3.11
, the functor

\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}
transforms (
23
)
into a split short exact sequence in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
(because
the projective system of finite quotient complexes of canonical
filtration of the complex
Y_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is termwise stabilizing).
Since, by the definition of an exact DG-category, the functor

\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}
reflects short exact sequences, it follows that
the short sequence (
23
)
is exact in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
.
Thus the totalization of (
23
)
is absolutely acyclic in
\mathbf{E}
.
As the middle and rightmost terms
of (
23
) are contraacyclic
in
\mathbf{E}
, it follows that the leftmost term is contraacyclic.
∎

Let us prove part (b) of the theorem (part (a) is dual).
Our aim is to show that for any object
B\in\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
there
exists a graded-projective object
P\in\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{proj}})
together
with a morphism
P\longrightarrow B
in
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
whose cone belongs
to
{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{E})
.

Let
B\in\mathbf{E}
be an object.
By Lemma
5.8
(b), there is
a short exact sequence
0\longrightarrow B_{1}\longrightarrow Q_{0}\longrightarrow B\longrightarrow 0

in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
with
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(Q_{0})\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{proj}}
.
Applying the same lemma to the object
B_{1}\in\mathbf{E}
, we obtain
a short exact sequence
0\longrightarrow B_{2}\longrightarrow Q_{1}\longrightarrow B_{1}\longrightarrow 0

in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
with
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(Q_{1})\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{proj}}
, etc.
Proceeding in this way, we construct an exact complex

\dotsb\longrightarrow Q_{2}\longrightarrow Q_{1}\longrightarrow Q_{0}\longrightarrow B\longrightarrow 0

in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
with
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(Q_{i})\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{proj}}
.

Denote by
T=\Tot^{\sqcap}(Q_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
the product totalization of
the complex
\dotsb\longrightarrow Q_{2}\longrightarrow Q_{1}\longrightarrow Q_{0}\longrightarrow 0

in the DG-category
\mathbf{E}
.
By Lemma
3.11
, the object
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(T)

is isomorphic to the product
\prod_{i=0}^{\infty}\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(Q_{i})[-i]

in the additive category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.
Hence condition (
**
) tells that the object
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(T)
has
finite projective dimension in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.
Let us denote this projective dimension by
n\geq 0
.
The cone of the natural morphism
T\longrightarrow B
in
\mathbf{E}
is
the product totalization of the bounded above exact complex

\dotsb\longrightarrow Q_{2}\longrightarrow Q_{1}\longrightarrow Q_{0}\longrightarrow B\longrightarrow 0

in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
, so it is contraacyclic by
Lemma
5.11
(b).

Now we restart the resolution procedure following the construction
from the proof of
Theorem
5.6
.
By Lemma
5.8
(b), there is
a short exact sequence
0\longrightarrow T_{1}\longrightarrow P_{0}\longrightarrow T\longrightarrow 0

in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
with
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(P_{0})\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{proj}}
.
Applying the same lemma to the object
T_{1}\in\mathbf{E}
, we obtain
a short exact sequence
0\longrightarrow T_{2}\longrightarrow P_{1}\longrightarrow T_{1}\longrightarrow 0

in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
with
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(P_{1})\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{proj}}
, etc.
Proceeding in this way, we construct an exact complex

0\longrightarrow Q\longrightarrow P_{n-1}\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow P_{0}\longrightarrow T\longrightarrow 0

in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
with
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(P_{i})\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{proj}}
.
Then the complex
0\longrightarrow\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(Q)\longrightarrow\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(P_{n-1})\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(P_{0})\longrightarrow\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(T)\longrightarrow 0
is
exact in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
, and it follows that

\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(Q)\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{proj}}
.

Put
P_{n}=Q
and
P=\Tot(P_{n}\to P_{n-1}\to\dotsb\to P_{1}\to P_{0})\in\mathbf{E}
.
Once again, by Lemma
3.11
, the object

\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(P)
is isomorphic to
\bigoplus_{i=0}^{\infty}\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(P_{i})[-i]
, hence it is projective in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.
The cone of the natural closed morphism
P\longrightarrow T
in
\mathbf{E}
is
the totalization of the finite exact complex
0\longrightarrow P_{n}\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow P_{0}\longrightarrow T\longrightarrow 0
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
, so it is absolutely
acyclic by Lemma
5.9
.

Finally, the cone of the composition
P\longrightarrow T\longrightarrow B
is
contraacyclic since both the cones of the morphisms
P\longrightarrow T

and
T\longrightarrow B
are contraacyclic in
\mathbf{E}
.
∎

Remark 5.12
.

All the results of
Sections
5.2
–
5.4

can be extended rather straightforwardly to the setting of

exact DG-pairs

(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
introduced below in
Section
6.1
.
In this context, one says that an object
P\in\mathbf{E}
is graded-projective
with respect to
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
if the object
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathsf{K}}(P)
is
projective in the exact category
\mathsf{K}
(and simililarly for
graded-injective objects with respect to
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
), etc.

6.
Finite Resolution Dimension Theorem

6.1.
Exact DG-pairs

The natural generality for the main results of
Sections
6
–
8

seems to be slightly wider than that of exact DG-categories.
In this connection, we introduce the following definition.

An
exact DG-pair

(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
is a pair consisting of
an exact DG-category
\mathbf{E}
and a full subcategory
\mathsf{K}\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
satisfying the following conditions:

•

the full subcategory
\mathsf{K}\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
is preserved
by the shift functors
[1]
and
[-1]
;

•

the image of the exact functor
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
is contained in
\mathsf{K}
;

•

the full subcategory
\mathsf{K}
inherits an exact category
structure from the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
.

We will denote the functor
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}
, viewed as taking values
in
\mathsf{K}
, by
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathsf{K}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{K}
.
The restrictions of the functors
\Psi_{\mathbf{E}}^{+}
and
\Psi_{\mathbf{E}}^{-}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
to the full subcategory

\mathsf{K}\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
will be denoted by
\Psi_{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathsf{K},+}

and
\Psi_{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathsf{K},-}\colon\mathsf{K}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
.

Let
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
be an exact DG-pair.
An
exact DG-subpair

(\mathbf{F},\mathsf{L})\subset(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
consists
of a full DG-subcategory
\mathbf{F}\subset\mathbf{E}
and a full subcategory

\mathsf{L}\subset\mathsf{K}
such that

•

the full subcategory
\mathsf{L}\subset\mathsf{K}
is preserved by
the shift functors
[1]
and
[-1
];

•

the full subcategory
\mathsf{L}\subset\mathsf{K}\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})

is contained in the full subcategory
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}^{\natural})\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
, that is
\mathsf{L}\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}^{\natural})
;

•

the image of the composition of the inclusion functor

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F})\rightarrowtail\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
with the functor

\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathsf{K}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{K}
is contained in
\mathsf{L}
;

•

the full DG-subcategory
\mathbf{F}
inherits an exact DG-category
structure from the exact DG-category
\mathbf{E}
;

•

the full subcategory
\mathsf{L}
inherits an exact category
structure from the exact category
\mathsf{K}
.

Clearly, if
(\mathbf{F},\mathsf{L})
is an exact DG-subpair in
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
,
then
(\mathbf{F},\mathsf{L})
is an exact DG-pair.

An exact DG-subpair
(\mathbf{F},\mathsf{L})\subset(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
is said to be

strict
if (
\mathsf{L}
is a strictly full subcategory in
\mathsf{K}
and)

\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathsf{K}}(F)\in\mathsf{L}
implies
F\in\mathbf{F}
for any given
object
F\in\mathbf{E}
.

Examples 6.1
.

(0) If
\mathbf{E}
is an exact DG-category, then
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural}))

is an exact DG-pair.
If a DG-subcategory
\mathbf{F}\subset\mathbf{E}
inherits an exact DG-category
structure from an exact DG-category
\mathbf{E}
, then
(\mathbf{F},\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}^{\natural}))

is an exact DG-subpair in
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural}))
.

Any exact DG-pair
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
is a strict exact DG-subpair in

(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural}))
.

(1) Let
\mathbf{E}
be an exact DG-category and
\mathsf{L}\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})

be a full subcategory preserved by the shift functors
[n]
,

n\in\Gamma
, and inheriting an exact category structure.
Consider the full DG-subcategory
\mathbf{F}\subset\mathbf{E}
whose objects are
all
F\in\mathbf{E}
for which
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(F)\in\mathsf{L}
, as in
Proposition
4.27
.
Then, by part (c) of the proposition,
\mathbf{F}
inherits an exact
DG-category structure from
\mathbf{E}
; in particular, the full subcategory

\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}=\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}^{\natural})\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
inherits
an exact category structure.
Hence the intersection
\mathsf{L}\cap\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})

inherits an exact category structure as well.
Thus
(\mathbf{F},\mathsf{L}\cap\widetilde{\mathsf{L}})
is a strict exact DG-subpair in

(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural}))
.
In particular, if
\mathsf{L}\subset\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}
(e. g., by part (d) of
the proposition, this holds whenever the full subcategory
\mathsf{L}
is
closed under extensions in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
), then

(\mathbf{F},\mathsf{L})
is a strict exact DG-subpair in
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural}))
.

(2) More generally, let
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
be an exact DG-pair, and let

\mathsf{L}\subset\mathsf{K}
be a full subcategory preserved by the shift
functors and inheriting an exact category structure.
Let
\mathbf{F}\subset\mathbf{E}
be the full DG-subcategory whose objects are
all
F\in\mathbf{E}
for which
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathsf{K}}(F)\in\mathsf{L}
.
Then (1) implies that
(\mathbf{F},\mathsf{L}\cap\widetilde{\mathsf{L}})
is a strict
exact DG-subpair in
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
.
Here
\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}=\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}^{\natural})\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
is
the full subcategory consisting of all objects
\widetilde{L}\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
such that
\Xi_{\mathbf{E}^{\natural}}(\widetilde{L})\in\mathsf{L}
,
as in Proposition
4.27
.

Assume that the full subcategory
\mathsf{L}
is closed under extensions
in
\mathsf{K}
.
Then, for any
L\in\mathsf{L}
, the short sequence
0\longrightarrow L[1]\longrightarrow\Xi_{\mathbf{E}^{\natural}}(L)\longrightarrow L\longrightarrow 0
is admissible exact in

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
with all the three terms belonging to
\mathsf{K}
.
It follows that
\Xi_{\mathbf{E}^{\natural}}(L)\in\mathsf{L}
and therefore

\mathsf{L}\subset\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}
.
Thus
(\mathbf{F},\mathsf{L})
is a strict exact DG-subpair in
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
.

(3) This example illustrates how the notion of an exact DG-pair
can be a nontrivial generalization of that of an exact DG-category.
Let
\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=(R^{*},d)
be the DG-ring from
Example
4.28
, that is
R^{*}=k[\epsilon]
with

\deg\epsilon=-1
,
\epsilon^{2}=0
, and
d(\epsilon)=1
.
Let
\mathbf{A}=\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}}
be the abelian DG-category of DG-modules
over
\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Following Example
3.17
,
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})\simeq R^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
is the abelian category of graded
R^{*}
-modules.
Let
\mathsf{K}\subset R^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
be the full subcategory of free
(equivalently, flat or projective) graded
R^{*}
-modules.
Following the discussion in Example
4.28
,
the underlying graded
R^{*}
-module of any DG-module over
\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

belongs to
\mathsf{K}
.
Hence
(\mathbf{A},\mathsf{K})
is an exact DG-pair; in fact, it is a strict exact
DG-subpair in the exact DG-pair
(\mathbf{A},\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural}))
.

(4) Let
\mathsf{E}
be an exact category.
Consider the DG-category of complexes
\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E})
, and endow it
with the exact DG-category structure constructed in
Example
4.39
.

Let the category of graded objects
\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{E})
be endowed with
the exact category structure constructed in the same example.
The category
\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{E})
can be viewed as a full subcategory in

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E})^{\natural})
embedded by the functor
\Upsilon_{\mathsf{E}}
.
Then
(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E}),\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{E}))
is an exact DG-pair.

Let
\mathsf{F}\subset\mathsf{E}
be a full subcategory inheriting an exact
category structure.
Then
(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{F}),\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{F}))
is a strict exact DG-subpair in

(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E}),\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{E}))
.

(5) Let
\mathsf{E}
be an exact category and
\Lambda\colon\mathsf{E}\longrightarrow\mathsf{E}

be an autoequivalence preserving and reflecting short exact
sequences.
Assume that either
\Gamma=\mathbb{Z}
, or
\Lambda
is involutive and

\Gamma=\mathbb{Z}/2
.
Let
w\colon\mathrm{Id}_{\mathsf{E}}\longrightarrow\Lambda^{2}
be a potential.
Consider the DG-category of factorizations
\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w)

and endow it with the exact DG-category structure constructed
in Example
4.42
.

Let the category of
\Lambda
-periodic objects
