\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda)

be endowed with the exact category structure constructed in
the same example.
The category
\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda)
can be viewed as a full subcategory
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w)^{\natural})
embedded by the functor

\Upsilon_{\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w}
.
Then
(\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w),\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda))
is an exact DG-pair.

Let
\mathsf{H}\subset\mathsf{E}
be a full subcategory preserved by
the autoequivalences
\Lambda
and
\Lambda^{-1}
and inheriting
an exact category structure.
Then
(\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{H},\Lambda,w),\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{H},\Lambda))
is a strict exact
DG-subpair in
(\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w),\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda))
.

6.2.
Resolving subcategories and resolution dimension

Let
\mathsf{E}
be an exact category.
A full subcategory
\mathsf{F}\subset\mathsf{E}
is said to be
resolving

[
4
, Section 3]
,
[
57
, Section 2]
if the following
conditions hold:

•

\mathsf{F}
is closed under extensions in
\mathsf{E}
;

•

\mathsf{F}
is closed under the kernels of admissible epimorphisms
in
\mathsf{E}
;

•

for every object
E\in\mathsf{E}
there exists an admissible
epimorphism
F\longrightarrow E
in
\mathsf{E}
with
F\in\mathsf{F}
.

Dually, a full subcategory
\mathsf{G}\subset\mathsf{E}
is said to be

coresolving
if

•

\mathsf{G}
is closed under extensions in
\mathsf{E}
;

•

\mathsf{G}
is closed under the cokernels of admissible monomorphisms
in
\mathsf{E}
;

•

for every object
E\in\mathsf{E}
there exists an admissible
monomorphism
E\longrightarrow G
in
\mathsf{E}
with
G\in\mathsf{G}
.

Obviously, any resolving or coresolving subcategory in an exact
category inherits an exact category structure (since it is closed
under extensions).

Assume that the exact category
\mathsf{E}
is weakly idempotent-complete
(in the sense of
[
14
, Section 7]
).
Clearly, then any resolving or coresolving subcategory in
\mathsf{E}
is
weakly idempotent-complete as well.

Let
\mathsf{F}\subset\mathsf{E}
be a resolving subcategory and
\mathsf{G}\subset\mathsf{E}

be a coresolving subcategory.
One says that the
resolution dimension

[
3
, Section 1]
,
[
57
, Section 2]
of an object
E\in\mathsf{E}

with respect to a resolving subcategory
\mathsf{F}\subset\mathsf{E}
is
\leq n

if there exists an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow F_{n}\longrightarrow F_{n-1}\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow F_{1}\longrightarrow F_{0}\longrightarrow E\longrightarrow 0
in
\mathsf{E}
with the terms
F_{i}\in\mathsf{F}
.
Dually, the
coresolution dimension
of an object
E\in\mathsf{E}

with respect to a coresolving subcategory
\mathsf{G}\subset\mathsf{E}
is
\leq n

if there exists an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow E\longrightarrow G^{0}\longrightarrow G^{1}\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow G^{n-1}\longrightarrow G^{n}\longrightarrow 0
in
\mathsf{E}
with the terms
G^{i}\in\mathsf{G}
.

Given an integer
n\geq 0
, denote by
\mathsf{F}_{n}\subset\mathsf{E}
the full
subcategory of all objects of resolution dimension
\leq n
with
respect to
\mathsf{F}
, and by
\mathsf{G}^{n}\subset\mathsf{E}
the full subcategory
of all objects of coresolution dimension
\leq n
with respect to
\mathsf{G}
.
In particular, for
n=0
we have
\mathsf{F}_{0}=\mathsf{F}
and
\mathsf{G}^{0}=\mathsf{G}
.

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
\mathsf{F}\subset\mathsf{E}
be a resolving subcategory and
\mathsf{G}\subset\mathsf{E}

be a coresolving subcategory.

(a)
Let
0\longrightarrow D\longrightarrow F_{n-1}\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow F_{0}\longrightarrow E\longrightarrow 0
be an exact complex in
\mathsf{E}
with
E\in\mathsf{F}_{n}

and
F_{i}\in\mathsf{F}
.
Then
D\in\mathsf{F}
.

(b)
Let
0\longrightarrow E\longrightarrow G^{0}\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow G^{n-1}\longrightarrow H\longrightarrow 0
be an exact complex in
\mathsf{E}
with
E\in\mathsf{G}^{n}

and
G^{i}\in\mathsf{G}
.
Then
H\in\mathsf{G}
.

Proof.

Part (a) is
[
57
, Proposition 2.3(1)]

or a particular case of
[
38
, Corollary A.5.2]
.
Part (b) is dual.
Alternatively, the assertions can be deduced by induction
from Proposition
6.4
below.
∎

Proposition 6.3
.

(a)
The full subcategory
\mathsf{F}_{n}
is resolving in
\mathsf{E}
.

(b)
The full subcategory
\mathsf{G}^{n}
is coresolving in
\mathsf{E}
.

Proof.

Part (a) is
[
57
, Proposition 2.3(2)]

or
[
38
, Lemma A.5.6(a–b)]
.
Part (b) is dual.
∎

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
0\longrightarrow E^{\prime}\longrightarrow E\longrightarrow E^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow 0
be a short exact
sequence in
\mathsf{E}
and
n\geq 0
be an integer.

(a)
If
E\in\mathsf{F}_{n}
and
E^{\prime\prime}\in\mathsf{F}_{n+1}
, then
E^{\prime}\in\mathsf{F}_{n}
.

(b)
If
E\in\mathsf{G}^{n}
and
E^{\prime}\in\mathsf{G}^{n+1}
, then
E^{\prime\prime}\in\mathsf{G}^{n}
.

Proof.

Part (a) is
[
38
, Lemma A.5.6(b)]
.
Part (b) is dual.
∎

6.3.
Finite resolution dimension theorem

We start with a generalization of
Lemma
5.8
.

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
be an exact DG-pair, and let
(\mathbf{F},\mathsf{L})
,

(\mathbf{G},\mathsf{M})\subset(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
be two exact DG-subpairs.

(a)
Assume that for every object
K\in\mathsf{K}
there exists
an admissible epimorphism
L\longrightarrow K
in the exact category
\mathsf{K}

with an object
L\in\mathsf{L}
.
Then for any object
E\in\mathbf{E}
there exists an admissible epimorphism

F\longrightarrow E
in the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
with
an object
F\in\mathbf{F}\subset\mathbf{E}
.

(b)
Assume that for every object
K\in\mathsf{K}
there exists
an admissible monomorphism
K\longrightarrow M
in the exact category
\mathsf{K}

with an object
M\in\mathsf{M}
.
Then for any object
E\in\mathbf{E}
there exists an admissible monomorphism

E\longrightarrow G
in the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
with
an object
G\in\mathbf{G}\subset\mathbf{E}
.

Proof.

Let us prove part (a) (part (b) is dual).
Given an object
E\in\mathbf{E}
, consider the object
\Phi(E)\in\mathsf{K}

and choose an object
L\in\mathsf{L}
together with an admissible
epimorphism
L\longrightarrow\Phi(E)
in
\mathsf{K}
.
By Lemma
5.7
(b), the related morphism

\Psi^{+}(L)\longrightarrow E
in an admissible epimorphism in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
.
Finally, we have
\Psi^{+}(L)\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F})
, because
L\in\mathsf{L}\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}^{\natural})
.
So it remains to put
F=\Psi^{+}(L)
.
∎

The idea of the following theorem, which is the main result of
Section
6
, goes back
to
[
34
, Theorem 7.2.2(a)]
,
[
33
, Theorem 3.2(a)]
,

[
18
, Theorem 1.4]
, and
[
38
, Proposition A.5.8]

(see also
[
49
, Theorem 5.5]
).
We use this opportunity to correct a small mistake in the proof
of
[
18
, Theorem 1.4]
related to the difference between
the thick and the full triangulated subcategory in
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})

generated by the totalizations of short exact sequences
(i. e., the adjoining of direct summands).
The proof of this theorem occupies the rest of
Section
6.3
.

Theorem 6.6
.

Let
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
be an exact DG-pair with a weakly idempotent-complete
additive category
\mathsf{K}
.
Let
(\mathbf{F},\mathsf{L})
,
(\mathbf{G},\mathsf{M})\subset(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
be two strict exact
DG-subpairs.

(a)
Assume that the full subcategory
\mathsf{L}
is resolving in
the exact category
\mathsf{K}
and all the objects of
\mathsf{K}
have finite
resolution dimensions with respect to
\mathsf{L}
.
Then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})

induced by the inclusion of exact DG-categories
\mathbf{F}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{E}
is an equivalence of triangulated categories.

Furthermore, if the exact DG-category
\mathbf{E}
has exact coproducts,
the full DG-subcategory
\mathbf{F}\subset\mathbf{E}
is closed under
coproducts, and the additive subcategories
\mathsf{L}
,
\mathsf{K}\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
are closed under coproducts, then
the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{F})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{E})

induced by the inclusion of exact DG-categories
\mathbf{F}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{E}
is a triangulated equivalence.
If the exact DG-category
\mathbf{E}
has exact products,
the full DG-subcategory
\mathbf{F}\subset\mathbf{E}
is closed under products,
and the additive subcategories
\mathsf{L}
,
\mathsf{K}\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
are
closed under products, then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{F})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{E})

induced by the inclusion
\mathbf{F}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{E}
is a triangulated
equivalence.

(b)
Assume that the full subcategory
\mathsf{M}
is coresolving in
the exact category
\mathsf{K}
and all the objects of
\mathsf{K}
have finite
coresolution dimensions with respect to
\mathsf{M}
.
Then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{G})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})

induced by the inclusion of exact DG-categories
\mathbf{G}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{E}
is an equivalence of triangulated categories.

Furthermore, if the exact DG-category
\mathbf{E}
has exact coproducts,
the full DG-subcategory
\mathbf{G}\subset\mathbf{E}
is closed under
coproducts, and the additive subcategories
\mathsf{M}
,
\mathsf{K}\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
are closed under coproducts, then
the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{G})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{E})

induced by the inclusion of exact DG-categories
\mathbf{G}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{E}
is a triangulated equivalence.
If the exact DG-category
\mathbf{E}
has exact products,
the full DG-subcategory
\mathbf{G}\subset\mathbf{E}
is closed under products,
and the additive subcategories
\mathsf{M}
,
\mathsf{K}\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
are
closed under products, then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{G})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{E})

induced by the inclusion
\mathbf{G}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{E}
is a triangulated
equivalence.

Proof.

Let us prove part (a) (part (b) is dual).
First of all, we show that for any object
E\in\mathbf{E}
there exists
an object
F\in\mathbf{F}
together with a morphism
F\longrightarrow E
in

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
whose cone belongs to
{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})
.

By Lemma
6.5
(a), there
is a short exact sequence
0\longrightarrow E_{1}\longrightarrow F_{0}\longrightarrow E\longrightarrow 0

in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
with
F_{0}\in\mathbf{F}
.
Applying the same lemma to the object
E_{1}\in\mathbf{E}
, we obtain
a short exact sequence
0\longrightarrow E_{2}\longrightarrow F_{1}\longrightarrow E_{1}\longrightarrow 0

in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
with
F_{1}\in\mathbf{F}
.
Proceeding in this way, we construct an exact complex
0\longrightarrow D\longrightarrow F_{n-1}\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow F_{0}\longrightarrow E\longrightarrow 0
in

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
with
F_{i}\in\mathbf{F}
, where
n
is the resolution dimension
of the object
\Phi(E)\in\mathsf{K}
with respect to the subcategory

\mathsf{L}\subset\mathsf{K}
.
Then the complex
0\longrightarrow\Phi(D)\longrightarrow\Phi(F_{n-1})\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow\Phi(F_{0})\longrightarrow\Phi(E)\longrightarrow 0
is exact in
\mathsf{K}
with

\Phi(F_{i})\in\mathsf{L}
.
By Proposition
6.2
(a), it follows that

\Phi(D)\in\mathsf{L}
.

By the assumption that
(\mathbf{F},\mathsf{L})
is a strict exact DG-subpair
in
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
, we can conclude that
D\in\mathbf{F}
.
Put
F_{n}=D
, and denote by
F
the totalization of the finite
complex
F_{n}\longrightarrow F_{n-1}\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow F_{0}
in
the DG-category
\mathbf{F}
.
Then the cone of the natural closed morphism
F\longrightarrow E
in
the DG-category
\mathbf{E}
is the totalization of the finite exact
complex
0\longrightarrow F_{n}\longrightarrow F_{n-1}\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow F_{0}\longrightarrow E\longrightarrow 0
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
; so it is absolutely acyclic in
\mathbf{E}

by Lemma
5.9
.

Now we apply the following lemma.

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category and
\mathsf{S}
,
\mathsf{X}\subset\mathsf{T}

be (strictly) full triangulated subcategories.
Assume that for every object
T\in\mathsf{T}
there exists an object
S\in\mathsf{S}

together with a morphism
S\longrightarrow T
in
\mathsf{T}
with a cone
belonging to
\mathsf{X}
.
Then the triangulated functor between the Verdier quotient categories

\mathsf{S}/(\mathsf{X}\cap\mathsf{S})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{T}/\mathsf{X}

induced by the inclusion of triangulated categories

\mathsf{S}\rightarrowtail\mathsf{T}
is an equivalence of triangulated categories.

Proof.

See
[
35
, Lemma 1.6(a)]
.
∎

In view of Lemma
6.7
and
the construction in the preceding paragraphs, in order
to prove the equivalence
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})
, it
suffices to check that

(24)

{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})\cap\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{F})={\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F})\quad\text{in $\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})$.}

Similarly, in order to prove the equivalence
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{F})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{E})
, it suffices to check that

(25)

{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{E})\cap\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{F})={\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{F})\quad\text{in $\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})$,}

while in order to prove the equivalence
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{F})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{E})
, it suffices to check that

(26)

{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{E})\cap\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{F})={\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{F})\quad\text{in $\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})$}

(under the respective assumptions).

For any integer
n\geq 1
, let
\mathsf{L}_{n}\subset\mathsf{K}
denote the full
subcategory of objects of resolution dimension
\leq n
with respect
to the resolving subcategory
\mathsf{L}
in the exact category
\mathsf{K}
.
By Proposition
6.3
,
\mathsf{L}_{n}

is a resolving subcategory in
\mathsf{K}
; in particular, the full
subcategory
\mathsf{L}_{n}\subset\mathsf{K}
is closed under extensions.
Following Example
6.1
(2), consider
the full DG-subcategory
\mathbf{F}_{n}\subset\mathbf{E}
consisting of all
the objects
E\in\mathbf{E}
such that
\Phi(E)\in\mathsf{L}_{n}
.
Then
(\mathbf{F}_{n},\mathsf{L}_{n})
is a strict exact DG-subpair in
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
.

Recall that
\mathsf{L}_{n}
is a resolving subcategory in
\mathsf{K}
by
Proposition
6.3
(a).
Taking into account
Lemma
6.5
(a),
we can conclude that
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
is a resolving subcategory
in the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
(since
(\mathbf{F}_{n},\mathsf{L}_{n})
is
a strict exact DG-subpair).
Moreover, in view of Proposition
6.4
(a),
the kernel of any admissible epimorphism in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
from
an object of
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
to an object of
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})

belongs to
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
.

In particular, for
n=0
we have
\mathsf{L}_{0}=\mathsf{L}
, hence
\mathbf{F}_{0}=\mathbf{F}
.
By assumption,
\bigcup_{n\geq 0}\mathsf{L}_{n}=\mathsf{K}
, hence
\bigcup_{n\geq 0}\mathbf{F}_{n}=\mathbf{E}
.
It follows that
{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})=\bigcup_{n\geq 0}{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n})\subset\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
.

In order to prove (
24
), we will show that

(27)

{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n})\cap\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})={\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})

for every
n\geq 1
.
Then it will follow by induction in
n
that
{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n})\cap\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{F})={\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F})
.

Let us say that an object
C\in\mathbf{F}_{n}
is

absolutely
(n-1)
-resolvable
if there exists a short exact
sequence
0\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow B\longrightarrow C\longrightarrow 0
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})

with
A
,
B\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
.
Let us emphasize that it is presumed here that
A
and
B
are
objects of
\mathbf{F}_{n-1}
(and
not
only homotopy equivalent to
such objects).
The following lemma is intended to express the idea of our approach.

Lemma 6.8
.

If an object
C\in\mathbf{F}_{n-1}
is absolutely
(n-1)
-resolvable,
then it belongs to
{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
.

Proof.

By assumptions, all the three terms of the short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow B\longrightarrow C\longrightarrow 0
belong to
\mathbf{F}_{n-1}
.
So we have
\Tot(A\to B\to C)\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
and
A
,

B\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
, hence also
C\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
.
∎

Our aim is to show that, for any object
D\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
,
the object
D\oplus D[1]\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
is
absolutely
(n-1)
-resolvable.
In view of Lemma
6.8
and
the discussion above, this will
imply (
27
), hence
also (
24
).

The full subcategory
{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
of absolutely acyclic objects
in
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
is defined by a generation procedure, and
the argument proceeds along the steps of this procedure.
We present it as a sequence of five lemmas.

Lemma 6.9
.

The total object of any short exact sequence in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})

is absolutely
(n-1)
-resolvable.

Proof.

This is our version of
[
33
, Lemma 3.2.A]
.
Let
0\longrightarrow E^{\prime}\longrightarrow E\longrightarrow E^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow 0
be a short exact
sequence in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
.
Choose two objects
F^{\prime}
,
F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbf{F}
together with admissible
epimorphisms
F^{\prime}\longrightarrow E^{\prime}
and
F^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow E
in the exact
category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
(or equivalently, in the exact category

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
).
Then there is a termwise admissible epimorphism from the split short
exact sequence
0\longrightarrow F^{\prime}\longrightarrow F^{\prime}\oplus F^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow F^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow 0

in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F})
to the short exact sequence
0\longrightarrow E^{\prime}\longrightarrow E\longrightarrow E^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow 0
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
.
The sequence of kernels of this termwise admissible epimorphism of
short exact sequences in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
is a short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow G^{\prime}\longrightarrow G\longrightarrow G^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow 0
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
.
Finally, we have a short exact sequence
0\longrightarrow\Tot(G^{\prime}\to G\to G^{\prime\prime})\longrightarrow\Tot(F^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}\oplus F^{\prime\prime}\to F^{\prime\prime})\longrightarrow\Tot(E^{\prime}\to E\to E^{\prime\prime})\longrightarrow 0
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
with
\Tot(G^{\prime}\to G\to G^{\prime\prime})\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
and
\Tot(F^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}\oplus F^{\prime\prime}\to F^{\prime\prime})\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F})
.
∎

Lemma 6.10
.

(a)
The class of all absolutely
(n-1)
-resolvable objects
is preserved by the cones of closed morphisms in
\mathbf{F}_{n}
.

(b)
The class of all absolutely
(n-1)
-resolvable objects
is preserved by the cokernels of admissible monomorphisms in

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
.

Proof.

We follow the argument from
[
33
, Lemma 3.2.B]
.
Let
0\longrightarrow A^{\prime}\longrightarrow B^{\prime}\longrightarrow C^{\prime}\longrightarrow 0
and
0\longrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow B^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow C^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow 0
be two short exact sequences in

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
with
A^{\prime}
,
B^{\prime}
,
A^{\prime\prime}
,
B^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
,
and let
C^{\prime}\longrightarrow C^{\prime\prime}
be a morphism in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
.
Put
B^{\prime\prime\prime}=B^{\prime}\oplus B^{\prime\prime}
, and consider two commutative diagrams of
morphisms of short exact sequences in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
,

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(1,0)}
\textstyle{C^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\prime\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B^{\prime\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

and

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(0,1)}
\textstyle{C^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\prime\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B^{\prime\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

where, on both the diagrams,
B^{\prime\prime\prime}\longrightarrow C^{\prime\prime}
is the morphism
whose components are the composition
B^{\prime}\longrightarrow C^{\prime}\longrightarrow C^{\prime\prime}

and the admissible epimorphism
B^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow C^{\prime\prime}
.
The composition
B^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow B^{\prime\prime\prime}\longrightarrow C^{\prime\prime}
is an admissible
epimorphism, while the direct summand inclusion
B^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow B^{\prime\prime\prime}

is admissible monomorphism; hence the morphism
B^{\prime\prime\prime}\longrightarrow C^{\prime\prime}
is
an admissible epimorphism in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
by the dual version
of
[
14
, first assertion of Exercise 3.11(i)]
, and we denote
by
A^{\prime\prime\prime}
its kernel.
Then there is an admissible short exact sequence
0\longrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow A^{\prime\prime\prime}\longrightarrow B^{\prime}\longrightarrow 0
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
with
A^{\prime\prime}
,
B^{\prime}\in\mathbf{F}_{n-1}
.
Therefore, we have
A^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathbf{F}_{n-1}
.
Moreover, since
A^{\prime\prime}
,
B^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
and

\Tot(A^{\prime\prime}\to A^{\prime\prime\prime}\to B^{\prime})\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
, it follows that

A^{\prime\prime\prime}\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
.

To prove part (a), denote by
A
and
B
the cones of the closed
morphisms
A^{\prime}\longrightarrow A^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
B^{\prime}\longrightarrow B^{\prime\prime\prime}
, respectively.
Then we have a short exact sequence
0\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow B\longrightarrow\cone(C^{\prime}\to C^{\prime\prime})\to 0
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
with
A
,

B\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
, showing that
\cone(C^{\prime}\to C^{\prime\prime})
is
absolutely
(n-1)
-resolvable.

To prove part (b), assume that
C^{\prime}\longrightarrow C^{\prime\prime}
is an admissible
monomorphism in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
with a cokernel
C_{0}\in\mathbf{F}_{n}
.
Then we have a morphism of (vertical) short exact sequences
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
depicted in the right-hand side of
the commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(1,0)}
\textstyle{C^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\prime\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B^{\prime\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(0,1)}
\textstyle{C^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

Here the lower rightmost horizontal morphism
B^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow C_{0}
is
the composition of admissible epimorphisms
B^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow C^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow C_{0}
,
so it is an admissible epimorphism as well.
Thus the morphism from the middle to the rightmost column is
a termwise admissible epimorphism of short exact sequences, and
therefore its kernel
0\longrightarrow A^{\prime}\longrightarrow A^{\prime\prime\prime}\longrightarrow A_{0}\longrightarrow 0

is a short exact sequence in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
, too.

Now we know that
B_{0}=B^{\prime\prime}\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
and
C_{0}\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
,
hence
A_{0}\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
.
Consequently,
0\longrightarrow A^{\prime}\longrightarrow A^{\prime\prime\prime}\longrightarrow A_{0}\longrightarrow 0
is
a short exact sequence in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
.
In this short exact sequence, we have
A^{\prime}
,
A^{\prime\prime\prime}\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
and
\Tot(A^{\prime}\to A^{\prime\prime\prime}\to A_{0})\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
, hence
A_{0}\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
.
Finally, the short exact sequence
0\longrightarrow A_{0}\longrightarrow B_{0}\longrightarrow C_{0}\longrightarrow 0
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
with
A_{0}
,
B_{0}\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})

shows that the object
C_{0}\in\mathbf{F}_{n}
is absolutely
(n-1)
-resolvable.
∎

Lemma 6.11
.

All contractible objects in
\mathbf{F}_{n}
are absolutely

(n-1)
-resolvable.

Proof.

We follow the argument from
[
18
, Lemma 1.4.C]
.
Let
C
be a contractible object in the DG-category
\mathbf{F}_{n}
with
a contracting homotopy
\sigma\in\Hom^{-1}_{\mathbf{E}}(C,C)
,

d(\sigma)=\mathrm{id}_{C}
.
Following (the proof of)
Lemma
6.5
(a),
for any object
L\in\mathsf{L}
endowed with an admissible epimorphism

p\colon L\longrightarrow\Phi(C)
in the exact category
\mathsf{L}_{n}
, the corresponding
(by adjunction) morphism
\tilde{p}\colon\Psi^{+}(L)\longrightarrow C
is
an admissible epimorphism in the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
.
Then we have
\Psi^{+}(L)\in\mathbf{F}
.
Our aim is to show that the kernel
A
of the admissible
epimorphism
\tilde{p}
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
belongs to

{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1}
).
This will immediately imply the assertion of the lemma.

By the definitions of the DG-category
\mathbf{E}^{\natural}
and
the additive functor
\Psi^{+}
, for any object
X^{\natural}\in\mathbf{E}^{\natural}
,
the object
\Psi^{+}(X^{\natural})\in\mathbf{E}
comes endowed with a contracting
homotopy
\sigma_{X}\in\Hom_{\mathbf{E}}^{-1}(\Psi^{+}(X^{\natural}),\Psi^{+}(X^{\natural}))
.
We are interested in the element
\sigma_{L}
, which is
the natural contracting homotopy for the object
\Psi^{+}(L)\in\mathbf{F}
.
Then
\tilde{u}=\tilde{p}\sigma_{L}-\sigma\tilde{p}\in\Hom^{-1}_{\mathbf{E}}(\Psi^{+}(L),C)
is a closed morphism of degree
-1

in the DG-category
\mathbf{F}_{n}
.

Denote by
u\colon L[-1]\longrightarrow\Phi(C)
the morphism in the additive
category
\mathsf{L}_{n}
corresponding by adjunction to the morphism

\tilde{u}\colon\Psi^{+}(L)[1]\longrightarrow C
in the additive category

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
(cf. Lemma
3.11
).
Consider the pullback of the admissible epimorphism
p\colon L\longrightarrow\Phi(C)
along the morphism
u\colon L[-1]\longrightarrow\Phi(C)
in the exact
category
\mathsf{L}_{n}
, and choose an admissible epimorphism onto
the resulting object from an object
M[-1]\in\mathsf{L}
.
We obtain a commutative square diagram

\textstyle{M[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q[-1]}
\scriptstyle{v}
\textstyle{L[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\Phi(C)}

with an admissible epimorphism
q\colon M\longrightarrow L
and some morphism

v\colon M[-1]\longrightarrow L
in the exact category
\mathsf{L}_{n}
.
Then it follows that
q
is an admissible epimorphism in
\mathsf{L}
.

The induced morphism
\tilde{q}=\Psi^{+}(q)\colon\Psi^{+}(M)\longrightarrow\Psi^{+}(L)
is an admissible epimorphism in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F})
.
The morphism
\tilde{q}
is homotopic to zero in the DG-category

\mathbf{F}
with the natural choice of the homotopy
\tilde{q}\sigma_{M}=\sigma_{L}\tilde{q}
.
Applying the functor
\Psi^{+}
to the morphism
v
, we obtain
the morphism
\tilde{v}=\Psi^{+}(v)\colon\Psi^{+}(M)[1]\longrightarrow\Psi^{+}(L)
,
which can be viewed as a closed morphism
\tilde{v}\in\Hom^{-1}_{\mathbf{E}}(\Psi^{+}(M),\Psi^{+}(L))
of degree
-1
in
the DG-category
\mathbf{F}
.
The morphism
\sigma_{L}\tilde{q}-\tilde{v}\in\Hom^{-1}_{\mathbf{E}}(\Psi^{+}(M),\Psi^{+}(L))
is another contracting
homotopy for the morphism
\tilde{q}
, i. e., we have

d(\sigma_{L}\tilde{q}-\tilde{v})=\tilde{q}
.
The contracting homotopy
\sigma_{L}\tilde{q}-\tilde{v}
also forms
a commutative square with the closed morphisms
\tilde{p}
,

\tilde{p}\tilde{q}
, and the contracting homotopy
\sigma

for the object
C\in\mathbf{F}_{n}
:

\textstyle{\Psi^{+}(M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{p}\tilde{q}}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{L}\tilde{q}-\tilde{v}}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{\Psi^{+}(L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{p}}
\textstyle{C}

Indeed,
\tilde{p}(\sigma_{L}\tilde{q}-\tilde{v})=(\tilde{p}\sigma_{L})\tilde{q}-\tilde{p}\tilde{v}=\sigma\tilde{p}\tilde{q}+\tilde{u}\tilde{q}-\tilde{p}\tilde{v}=\sigma\tilde{p}\tilde{q}
, as the equation
\tilde{u}\tilde{q}=\tilde{p}\tilde{v}
follows from
uq=pv
by adjunction.

Let
D\in\mathbf{F}_{n-1}
be the kernel of the admissible epimorphism

\tilde{p}\tilde{q}\colon\Psi^{+}(M)\longrightarrow C
and
A\in\mathbf{F}_{n-1}
be the kernel
of the admissible epimorphism
\tilde{p}\colon\Psi^{+}(L)\longrightarrow C
in
the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
(with
\Psi^{+}(M)
,

\Psi^{+}(L)\in\mathbf{F}
).
Then the natural admissible epimorphism
r\colon D\longrightarrow A
in
the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
is homotopic to zero in
the DG-category
\mathbf{F}_{n-1}
, with a contracting homotopy

\tau\in\Hom^{-1}_{\mathbf{E}}(D,A)
induced by the contracting homotopy

\sigma_{L}\tilde{q}-\tilde{v}\in\Hom^{-1}_{\mathbf{E}}(\Psi^{+}(M),\Psi^{+}(L))
:

\textstyle{D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r}
\textstyle{\Psi^{+}(M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{p}\tilde{q}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{q}}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Psi^{+}(L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{p}}
\textstyle{C}

\textstyle{D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau}
\textstyle{\Psi^{+}(M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{p}\tilde{q}}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{L}\tilde{q}-\tilde{v}}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Psi^{+}(L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{p}}
\textstyle{C}

Furthermore, the functor
\Psi^{+}\colon\mathsf{L}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F})
is exact,
so we have a short exact sequence
0\longrightarrow\Psi^{+}(\ker q)\longrightarrow\Psi^{+}(M)\longrightarrow\Psi^{+}(L)\longrightarrow 0
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F})
, and consequently
a short exact sequence
0\longrightarrow\Psi^{+}(\ker q)\longrightarrow D\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow 0
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
.
Taking the pullback of the latter short exact sequence along
the natural admissible epimorphism
\Xi_{\mathbf{E}}(A)\longrightarrow A
, we obtain
a short exact sequence

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Psi^{+}(\ker q)\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\cone(r)[-1]\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Xi_{\mathbf{E}}(A)\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0

in the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
(cf. the alternative proof
of Corollary
4.9
).
On the other hand, the closed morphism
r
is homotopic to zero,
so its cone is isomorphic to
A\oplus D[1]
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
.
Since the objects
\Psi^{+}(\ker q)
and
\Xi_{\mathbf{E}}(A)
are contractible
and
\Tot(\Psi^{+}(\ker q)\to\cone(r)[-1]\to\Xi_{\mathbf{E}}(A))\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
, it follows that
\cone(r)\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})

and therefore
A\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
.

Finally, the short exact sequence
0\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow\Psi^{+}(L)\longrightarrow C\longrightarrow 0
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
with
A\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
and

\Psi^{+}(L)
contractible in
\mathbf{F}
shows that the object
C\in\mathbf{F}_{n}

is absolutely
(n-1)
-resolvable, as desired.
∎

Lemma 6.12
.

The class of all absolutely
(n-1)
-resolvable objects in
\mathbf{F}_{n}

is preserved by the homotopy equivalences (i. e., the isomorphisms
in
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
).

Proof.

This is our version of
[
33
, Lemma 3.2.D]
.
Let
C\longrightarrow C^{\prime}
be a closed morphism which is a homotopy equivalence
in the DG-category
\mathbf{F}_{n}
such that the object
C^{\prime}
is absolutely

(n-1)
-resolvable.
Then the object
\cone(C\to C^{\prime})\in\mathbf{F}_{n}
is contractible, so it is
absolutely
(n-1)
-resolvable by
Lemma
6.11
.

By Lemma
6.10
(a), it follows that
the objects
\Xi_{\mathbf{E}}(C^{\prime})=\cone(\mathrm{id}_{C^{\prime}}[-1])
and

C^{\prime\prime}=\cone(C^{\prime}\to\cone(C\to C^{\prime}))[-1]\in\mathbf{F}_{n}
are also absolutely

(n-1)
-resolvable.
The object
C^{\prime\prime}
is isomorphic to the direct sum
C\oplus\Xi_{\mathbf{E}}(C^{\prime})

in the additive category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
.
Hence, by Lemma
6.10
(b), the cokernel

C
of the direct summand inclusion
\Xi_{\mathbf{E}}(C^{\prime})\longrightarrow C^{\prime\prime}
is
absolutely
(n-1)
-resolvable as well.
∎

Lemma 6.13
.

If
D
,
D^{\prime}\in\mathbf{F}_{n}
are two objects such that the object
D\oplus D^{\prime}

is homotopy equivalent to an absolutely
(n-1)
-resolvable object,
then the object
D\oplus D[1]
is absolutely
(n-1)
-resolvable.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.12
, the object

D\oplus D^{\prime}\in\mathbf{F}_{n}
itself is absolutely
(n-1)
-resolvable.
Consider the endomorphism
D\oplus D^{\prime}\longrightarrow D\oplus D^{\prime}

whose only nonzero component is the identity morphism

\mathrm{id}_{D^{\prime}}\colon D^{\prime}\longrightarrow D^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
6.10
(a), it follows that
the cone of this closed endomorphism of degree
0
in
\mathbf{F}_{n}

is absolutely
(n-1)
-resolvable.
This cone is homotopy equivalent to
D\oplus D[1]
; so applying
Lemma
6.12
again we conclude that
the object
D\oplus D[1]\in\mathbf{F}_{n}
is absolutely
(n-1)
-resolvable.
∎

The following lemma summarizes the results of the previous ones,
as it was promised before the beginning of this series of lemmas.

Lemma 6.14
.

For any object
D\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
, the object
D\oplus D[1]\in\mathbf{F}_{n}
is absolutely
(n-1)
-resolvable.

Proof.

Follows from Lemmas
6.9
,

6.10
(a),

6.12
,
and
6.13
by the definition
of the full subcategory of absolutely acyclic objects

{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n})\subset\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
.
∎

Now we can deduce the equality (
27
).
Let
D\in\mathbf{F}_{n-1}
be an object belonging to
{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
.
Then, by Lemma
6.14
,
the object
D\oplus D[1]\in\mathbf{F}_{n-1}
is absolutely

(n-\penalty 1)
-resolvable.
By Lemma
6.8
, we can conclude
that
D\oplus D[-1]\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
; hence

D\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
.
This finishes the proof of
the equalites (
27
)
and (
24
), and of part (a) of the theorem
for absolute derived categories.

We still have to prove part (a) for the coderived and contraderived
categories.
Concerning the coderived categories, assume that infinite coproducts
are well-behaved in the exact DG-pair
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
and its exact
DG-subpair
(\mathbf{F},\mathsf{L})
, as per the formulation of the theorem.
First of all, we observe that the full subcategories
\mathsf{L}_{n}\subset\mathsf{K}

of objects of resolution dimension
\leq n
with respect to
\mathsf{L}
are
closed under infinite coproducts in this case (since the coproducts
are exact in the exact category
\mathsf{K}
and the full subcategory

\mathsf{L}\subset\mathsf{K}
is closed under coproducts).
Consequently, the full DG-subcategories
\mathbf{F}_{n}\subset\mathbf{E}
are
closed under coproducts.

Furthermore, we claim that the resolution dimensions of objects
of the exact category
\mathsf{K}
with respect to its resolving
subcategory
\mathsf{L}
are uniformly bounded in this case, that is,
there is an integer
m\geq 0
such that
\mathsf{K}=\mathsf{L}_{m}
.
Indeed, suppose the contrary; then there exists a sequence of objects

(K_{n}\in\mathsf{K})_{n\geq 0}
such that
K_{n}\notin\mathsf{L}_{n}
.
Consider the object
K=\coprod_{n\geq 0}K_{n}\in\mathsf{K}
; by assumption,
there exists
m\geq 0
such that
K\in\mathsf{L}_{m}
.
However, the object
K_{n}
is a direct summand of
K
for every
n
.
In order to come to a contradiction, it remains to notice that
the full subcategory
\mathsf{L}_{m}\subset\mathsf{K}
is closed under direct summands,
as in fact any weakly idempotent-complete additive category with
countable coproducts, and
\mathsf{L}_{m}
in particular, is idempotent-complete
(by the cancellation trick).

In order to prove (
25
), one shows that

(28)

{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{F}_{n})\cap\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})={\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})

for every
n\geq 1
.
Then it follows by induction in
n
that
{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{F}_{n})\cap\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{F})={\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{F})
for all
0\leq n\leq m
.

Let us say that an object
C\in\mathbf{F}_{n}
is

coacycl-
(n-1)
-resolvable
if there exists a short exact
sequence
0\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow B\longrightarrow C\longrightarrow 0
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})

with
A
,
B\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
.
Similarly to the previous argument, it is presumed here that
A

and
B
are objects of
\mathbf{F}_{n-1}
(and
not
only homotopy
equivalent to such objects).
The proof is based on the following straightforward analogue
of Lemma
6.8
.

Lemma 6.15
.

If an object
C\in\mathbf{F}_{n-1}
is coacycl-
(n-1)
-resolvable,
then it belongs to
{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
. ∎

Lemma 6.16
.

The class of all coacycl-
(n-1)
-resolvable objects is closed
under infinite coproducts in
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
.

Proof.

Follows from the assumption that the coproducts are exact in
the exact category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
and the fact that the full
subcategory
{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})\subset\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
is
closed under coproducts by the definition.
∎

Lemma 6.17
.

(a)
The class of all coacycl-
(n-1)
-resolvable objects
is preserved by the cones of closed morphisms in
\mathbf{F}_{n}
.

(b)
The class of all coacycl-
(n-1)
-resolvable objects
is preserved by the cokernels of admissible monomorphisms in

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
.

Proof.

Similar to the proof of
Lemma
6.10
.
∎

Lemma 6.18
.

The class of all coacycl-
(n-1)
-resolvable objects in
\mathbf{F}_{n}

is preserved by the homotopy equivalences.

Proof.

Similar to the proof of
Lemma
6.12
.
∎

Lemma 6.19
.

All the objects in
{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
are coacycl-
(n-1)
-resolvable.

Proof.

Follows from Lemmas
6.9
,

6.17
(a),

6.16
,
and
6.18
by the definition
of the full subcategory of coacyclic objects

{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{F}_{n})\subset\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
.
∎

Now we can deduce the equality (
28
).
Let
C\in\mathbf{F}_{n-1}
be an object belonging to
{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{F}_{n})
.
Then, by Lemma
6.19
, the object
C

is coacycl-
(n-1)
-resolvable.
By Lemma
6.15
, it follows that
that
C\in{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})
, as desired.
This finishes the proof of
the equalites (
28
)
and (
25
), and of part (a) of the theorem
for coderived categories.

The argument for the contraderived categories in part (a), under
the respective assumptions that infinite products are well-behaved
in the exact DG-pair
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
and its exact DG-subpair

(\mathbf{F},\mathsf{L})
, is completely similar to the proof of the assertion for
the coderived categories above.
One defines
contraacycl-
(n-1)
-resolvable
objects in
\mathbf{F}_{n}
,
proves the respective versions of
Lemmas
6.15
–
6.19
, and deduces the desired
equality (
26
) from the equality

(29)

{\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{F}_{n})\cap\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})={\mathsf{Ac}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{F}_{n-1})

by induction in
0\leq n\leq m
, using the fact that there exists

m\geq 0
such that
\mathsf{K}=\mathsf{L}_{m}
.
We omit the straightforward details.

Alternatively, the assertion of part (a) for the contraderived
categories and the assertion of part (b) for the coderived categories
can be obtained as particular cases of the more general result of
Theorem
7.11
, which
does not require finite (co)resolution dimension.
Moreover, after we already know that the triangulated functor in
question is essentially surjective, which was explained in
the very beginning of this proof, it becomes enough to show that
the functor is fully faithful.
So the assertions of parts (a) and (b) for the absolute derived
categories can be deduced from the quite general result of
Theorem
7.9
, which does not
require finite (co)resolution dimension, either.

Notice, however, that the assertion of part (a) for the coderived
categories and the assertion of part (b) for the contraderived
categories do not seem to be provable in this alternative way
(cf.
[
18
, Remark 1.5]
).
∎

6.4.
Examples

In this section we formulate the particular cases of
Theorem
6.6
arising
in the context of Examples
4.39
–
4.42

and
6.1
.

For any exact category
\mathsf{E}
, we put
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathsf{E})=\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E}))
, where
\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E})
is the exact DG-category
of complexes in
\mathsf{E}
constructed in
Example
4.39
.
If the exact category
\mathsf{E}
has exact coproducts, then so does
the DG-category
\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E})
.
In this case, we put
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{E})=\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E}))
.
Dually, if the exact category
\mathsf{E}
has exact products, then
so does the DG-category
\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E})
, and in this case we put

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathsf{E})=\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E}))
.

The following result can be found in
[
38
, Proposition A.5.8]
.

Corollary 6.20
.

Let
\mathsf{E}
be an exact category and
\mathsf{F}
be
a resolving subcategory in
\mathsf{E}
such that all the objects of

\mathsf{E}
have finite resolution dimensions with respect to
\mathsf{F}
.
If the grading group
\Gamma
is infinite, assume additionally
that the
\mathsf{F}
-resolution dimensions of the objects of
\mathsf{E}

are uniformly bounded, i. e., there exists an integer
m\geq 0

such that the
\mathsf{F}
-resolution dimension of any object of
\mathsf{E}

does not exceed
m
.
Then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathsf{F})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathsf{E})

induced by the inclusion of exact categories
\mathsf{F}\rightarrowtail\mathsf{E}

is an equivalence of triangulated categories.

Furthermore, if the exact category
\mathsf{E}
has exact coproducts and
the full subcategory
\mathsf{F}\subset\mathsf{E}
is closed under coproducts,
then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{F})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{E})

induced by the inclusion of exact categories
\mathsf{F}\rightarrowtail\mathsf{E}

is a triangulated equivalence.
If the exact category
\mathsf{E}
has exact products and the full
subcategory
\mathsf{F}\subset\mathsf{E}
is closed under products, then
the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathsf{F})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathsf{E})

induced by the inclusion
\mathsf{F}\rightarrowtail\mathsf{E}
is a triangulated
equivalence.

Proof.

Put
\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{E})
,
\mathbf{F}=\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{F})
,
\mathsf{K}=\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{E})
,

\mathsf{L}=\mathsf{G}(\mathsf{F})
, as in Examples
4.39

and
6.1
(4), and apply
Theorem
6.6
(a).
∎

The following corollary is a generalization
of
[
33
, Theorem 3.2]
.

Corollary 6.21
.

Let
\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(R^{*},d,h)
be a CDG-ring and
\mathsf{K}\subset R^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
be
a full subcategory in the category of graded
R^{*}
-modules such that

\mathsf{K}
is preserved by the shift functors
[n]
,
n\in\Gamma
,
inherits an exact category structure from the abelian exact structure
of
R^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
, and the underlying graded
R^{*}
-module of
the CDG-module
G^{+}(K^{*})
belongs to
\mathsf{K}
for any
K^{*}\in\mathsf{K}
.
Denote by
\mathbf{E}\subset\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}}
the full DG-subcategory
consisting of all the left CDG-modules over
\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}

whose underlying graded
R^{*}
-modules belong to
\mathsf{K}
,
and endow
\mathbf{E}
with the exact DG-category structure inherited
from the abelian exact DG-category structure of
\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}}
.

(a)
Let
\mathsf{L}\subset\mathsf{K}
be a resolving subcategory
such that
\mathsf{L}
is preserved by the shift functors and all
the objects of
\mathsf{K}
have finite resolution dimensions
with respect to
\mathsf{L}
.
Denote by
\mathbf{F}\subset\mathbf{E}
the full DG-subcategory consisting
of all the CDG-modules whose underlying graded
R^{*}
-modules
belong to
\mathsf{L}
, and endow
\mathbf{F}
with the inherited exact
DG-category structure.
Then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})

induced by the inclusion of exact DG-categories
\mathbf{F}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{E}
is an equivalence of triangulated categories.

Furthermore, if the full subcategories
\mathsf{L}
and
\mathsf{K}
are
preserved by the infinite direct sums in
R^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
, then
the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{F})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{E})

induced by the inclusion of exact DG-categories
\mathbf{F}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{E}
is a triangulated equivalence.
If the full subcategories
\mathsf{L}
and
\mathsf{K}
are preserved by
the infinite products in
R^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
, then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{F})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{E})

induced by the inclusion
\mathbf{F}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{E}
is a triangulated
equivalence.

(b)
Let
\mathsf{M}\subset\mathsf{K}
be a coresolving subcategory
such that
\mathsf{M}
is preserved by the shift functors and all
the objects of
\mathsf{K}
have finite coresolution dimensions
with respect to
\mathsf{M}
.
Denote by
\mathbf{G}\subset\mathbf{E}
the full DG-subcategory consisting
of all the CDG-modules whose underlying graded
R^{*}
-modules
belong to
\mathsf{M}
, and endow
\mathbf{G}
with the inherited exact
DG-category structure.
Then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{G})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})

induced by the inclusion of exact DG-categories
\mathbf{G}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{E}
is an equivalence of triangulated categories.

Furthermore, if the full subcategories
\mathsf{M}
and
\mathsf{K}
are
preserved by the infinite direct sums in
R^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
, then
the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{G})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{E})

induced by the inclusion of exact DG-categories
\mathbf{G}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{E}
is a triangulated equivalence.
If the full subcategories
\mathsf{M}
and
\mathsf{K}
are preserved by
the infinite products in
R^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
, then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{G})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{E})

induced by the inclusion
\mathbf{G}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{E}
is a triangulated
equivalence.

Proof.

It was explained in Example
4.40

that the DG-category
\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}}
of left CDG-modules over

(R^{*},d,h)
is an abelian DG-category.
It follows from Proposition
4.27
(c) and
the discussion in Example
6.1
(1) that,
under the assumptions from the first paragraph of the corollary,

(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
is a strict exact DG-subpair in

(\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}},R^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}})
.
Furthermore, following Example
6.1
(2),
in the assumptions of part (a)
(\mathbf{F},\mathsf{L})
is a strict
exact DG-subpair in
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
, while in the assumptions of
part (b)
(\mathbf{G},\mathsf{M})
is a strict exact DG-subpair in
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
.
It remains to recall that both the infinite coproducts and
infinite products are exact in the abelian category
R^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}

in order to conclude that the assertions of parts (a) and (b) of
the corollary are particular cases of those of
Theorem
6.6
(a–b).
∎

Part (a) of the next corollary is a generalization
of
[
18
, Theorem 1.4]
.

Corollary 6.22
.

Let
X
be a scheme and
\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B^{*},d,h)
be a quasi-coherent
CDG-quasi-algebra over
X
.
Let
\mathsf{K}\subset B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--qcoh}}}
be a full subcategory in the category
of quasi-coherent graded
B^{*}
-modules such that
\mathsf{K}
is preserved
by the shift functors
[n]
,
n\in\Gamma
, inherits an exact category
structure from the abelian exact structure of
B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--qcoh}}}
, and
the underlying quasi-coherent graded
B^{*}
-module of the quasi-coherent
CDG-module
G^{+}(K^{*})
belongs to
\mathsf{K}
for any
K^{*}\in\mathsf{K}
.
Denote by
\mathbf{E}\subset\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--qcoh}}}
the full DG-subcategory
consisting of all the quasi-coherent left CDG-modules over
\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}

whose underlying quasi-coherent graded
B^{*}
-modules belong to
\mathsf{K}
,
and endow
\mathbf{E}
with the exact DG-category structure inherited
from the abelian exact DG-category structure of
\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--qcoh}}}
.

(a)
Let
\mathsf{L}\subset\mathsf{K}
be a resolving subcategory
such that
\mathsf{L}
is preserved by the shift functors and all
the objects of
\mathsf{K}
have finite resolution dimensions
with respect to
\mathsf{L}
.
Denote by
\mathbf{F}\subset\mathbf{E}
the full DG-subcategory consisting
of all the quasi-coherent CDG-modules whose underlying quasi-coherent
graded
B^{*}
-modules belong to
\mathsf{L}
, and endow
\mathbf{F}
with
the inherited exact DG-category structure.
Then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})

induced by the inclusion of exact DG-categories
\mathbf{F}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{E}
is an equivalence of triangulated categories.

Furthermore, if the full subcategories
\mathsf{L}
and
\mathsf{K}
are
preserved by the infinite direct sums in
B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--qcoh}}}
, then
the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{F})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{E})

induced by the inclusion of exact DG-categories
\mathbf{F}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{E}
is a triangulated equivalence.

(b)
Let
\mathsf{M}\subset\mathsf{K}
be a coresolving subcategory
such that
\mathsf{M}
is preserved by the shift functors and all
the objects of
\mathsf{K}
have finite coresolution dimensions
with respect to
\mathsf{M}
.
Denote by
\mathbf{G}\subset\mathbf{E}
the full DG-subcategory consisting
of all the quasi-coherent CDG-modules whose underlying quasi-coherent
graded
B^{*}
-modules belong to
\mathsf{M}
, and endow
\mathbf{G}
with
the inherited exact DG-category structure.
Then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{G})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{E})

induced by the inclusion of exact DG-categories
\mathbf{G}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{E}
is an equivalence of triangulated categories.

Furthermore, if the full subcategories
\mathsf{M}
and
\mathsf{K}
are
preserved by the infinite direct sums in
B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--qcoh}}}
, then
the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{G})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{E})

induced by the inclusion of exact DG-categories
\mathbf{G}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{E}
is a triangulated equivalence.

Proof.

According to
Example
4.41
,
the DG-category
\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--qcoh}}}
of quasi-coherent left CDG-modules over

(B^{*},d,h)
is an abelian DG-category.
It follows from Proposition
4.27
(c) and
the discussion in Example
6.1
(1) that,
under the assumptions from the first paragraph of the corollary,

(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
is a strict exact DG-subpair in

(\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--qcoh}}},B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--qcoh}}})
.
Following Example
6.1
(2), in the assumptions
of part (a)
(\mathbf{F},\mathsf{L})
is a strict exact DG-subpair in
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
,
while in the assumptions of part (b)
(\mathbf{G},\mathsf{M})
is a strict exact
DG-subpair in
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})
.
It remains to recall that the infinite coproducts are exact in
the abelian category
B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--qcoh}}}
and apply
Theorem
6.6
(a–b)
in order to deduce parts (a) and (b) of the corollary.
∎

Notice that assertions about the contraderived categories are missing
from
Corollary
6.22
(a–b),
because the functors of infinite product of quasi-coherent sheaves
are not exact.
If one wishes to assign some contraderived categories to
a quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebra
\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over a nonaffine
scheme
X
, then the way to proceed is to consider contraherent
cosheaves of CDG-modules over
\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
instead of quasi-coherent
sheaves (see
[
38
]
).

The following corollary is a generalization
of
[
18
, Corollaries 2.3(a,b,c,e,g) and 2.6]
.

Corollary 6.23
.

Let
\mathsf{E}
be an exact category and
\Lambda\colon\mathsf{E}\longrightarrow\mathsf{E}
be
an autoequivalence preserving and reflecting short exact sequences.
Assume that either
\Gamma=\mathbb{Z}
, or
\Lambda
is involutive
and
\Gamma=\mathbb{Z}/2
.
Let
w\colon\mathrm{Id}_{\mathsf{E}}\longrightarrow\Lambda^{2}
be a potential (as in
Section
2.5
).
Let
\mathsf{H}
be a resolving subcategory in
\mathsf{E}
preserved by
the autoequivalences
\Lambda
and
\Lambda^{-1}
and such that
all the objects of
\mathsf{E}
have finite resolution dimensions with
respect to
\mathsf{H}
.
Then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{H},\Lambda,w))\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w))

induced by the inclusion of exact categories
\mathsf{H}\rightarrowtail\mathsf{E}

is an equivalence of triangulated categories.

Furthermore, if the exact category
\mathsf{E}
has exact coproducts and
the full subcategory
\mathsf{H}\subset\mathsf{E}
is closed under coproducts,
then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{H},\Lambda,w))\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w))

induced by the inclusion of exact categories
\mathsf{H}\rightarrowtail\mathsf{E}

is a triangulated equivalence.
If the exact category
\mathsf{E}
has exact products and the full
subcategory
\mathsf{H}\subset\mathsf{E}
is closed under products, then
the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{H},\Lambda,w))\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w))

induced by the inclusion
\mathsf{H}\rightarrowtail\mathsf{E}
is a triangulated
equivalence.

Proof.

Put
\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda,w)
,
\mathbf{F}=\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{H},\Lambda,w)
,

\mathsf{K}=\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{E},\Lambda)
,
\mathsf{L}=\mathsf{P}(\mathsf{H},\Lambda)
,
as in Examples
4.42

and
6.1
(5), and apply
Theorem
6.6
(a).
∎

7.
Full-and-Faithfulness Theorem

7.1.
Self-resolving subcategories in exact categories

Let
\mathsf{E}
be an exact category.
The following condition is fairly well-known
(see
[
25
, Section 12]
), but we are not aware of any terminology
for it in the literature.
We will say that a full subcategory
\mathsf{F}\subset\mathsf{E}
is

self-resolving
if

•

\mathsf{F}
inherits an exact category structure from
\mathsf{E}
;

•

\mathsf{F}
is closed under the kernels of admissible epimorphisms
in
\mathsf{E}
;

•

for any objects
F\in\mathsf{F}
and
E\in\mathsf{E}
, and any admissible
epimorphism
E\longrightarrow F
in
\mathsf{E}
, there exist an object
F^{\prime}\in\mathsf{F}
,
an admissible epimorphism
F^{\prime}\longrightarrow F
, and a morphism
F^{\prime}\longrightarrow E

such that the triangle diagram
F^{\prime}\longrightarrow E\longrightarrow F
is commutative
in
\mathsf{E}
.

Clearly, any resolving subcategory is self-resolving.

Dually, a full subcategory
\mathsf{G}\subset\mathsf{E}
is said to be

self-coresolving
if

•

\mathsf{G}
inherits an exact category structure from
\mathsf{E}
;

•

\mathsf{G}
is closed under the cokernels of admissible monomorphisms
in
\mathsf{E}
;

•

for any objects
G\in\mathsf{G}
and
E\in\mathsf{E}
, and any admissible
monomorphism
G\longrightarrow E
in
\mathsf{E}
, there exist an object
G^{\prime}\in\mathsf{G}
,
an admissible monomorphism
G\longrightarrow G^{\prime}
, and a morphism
E\longrightarrow G^{\prime}

such that the triangle diagram
G\longrightarrow E\longrightarrow G^{\prime}
is commutative
in
\mathsf{E}
.

Clearly, any coresolving subcategory is self-coresolving.

The following lemma is our version of
[
48
, Remark 2.2]
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Any self-resolving or self-coresolving subcategory in an exact
category is closed under extensions.

Proof.

Let
0\longrightarrow G\longrightarrow E\longrightarrow F\longrightarrow 0
be a short exact sequence
in an exact category
\mathsf{E}
with the objects
F
and
G
belonging to
a self-resolving subcategory
\mathsf{F}\subset\mathsf{E}
.
Then there exists an object
F^{\prime}\in\mathsf{F}
, an admissible epimorphism

F^{\prime}\longrightarrow F
, and a morphism
F^{\prime}\longrightarrow E
such that the triangle
diagram
F^{\prime}\longrightarrow E\longrightarrow F
is commutative.
Since
\mathsf{F}
is closed under the kernels of admissible epimorphisms
in
\mathsf{E}
, the kernel
H
of the admissible epimorphism
F^{\prime}\longrightarrow F

belongs to
\mathsf{F}
.
So we have a commutative diagram in the exact category
\mathsf{E}

\textstyle{H\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F}
\textstyle{G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

with the objects
H
,
F^{\prime}
,
F
,
G\in\mathsf{F}
and admissible short
exact sequences
0\longrightarrow H\longrightarrow F^{\prime}\longrightarrow F\longrightarrow 0
and

0\longrightarrow G\longrightarrow E\longrightarrow F\longrightarrow 0
.
By
[
36
, Proposition A.2 and Corollary A.3]
, it follows that

H\longrightarrow F^{\prime}\longrightarrow E
,
H\longrightarrow G\longrightarrow E
is a pushout square
in
\mathsf{E}
.
Now Lemma
4.20
(ii) tells that

E\in\mathsf{F}
.
∎

7.2.
Approachability in triangulated categories

The terminology “approachable object” goes back
to
[
48
, proof of Theorem 5.5]
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category and
\mathsf{S}
,
\mathsf{Y}\subset\mathsf{T}
be
two full subcategories.
We will say that an object
X\in\mathsf{T}
is
approachable from

\mathsf{S}
via
\mathsf{Y}
if every morphism
S\longrightarrow X
in
\mathsf{T}
with
an object
S\in\mathsf{S}
factorizes through an object from
\mathsf{Y}
.

Equivalently, an object
X
is approachable from
\mathsf{S}
via
\mathsf{Y}
if
and only if, for every morphism
S\longrightarrow X
in
\mathsf{T}
with
S\in\mathsf{S}

there exists an object
S^{\prime}\in\mathsf{T}
and a morphism
S^{\prime}\longrightarrow S
in
\mathsf{T}

with a cone belonging to
\mathsf{Y}
such that the composition
S^{\prime}\longrightarrow S\longrightarrow X
vanishes in
\mathsf{T}
.
When
\mathsf{S}
is a full triangulated subcategory in
\mathsf{T}
and

\mathsf{Y}\subset\mathsf{S}
, the conditions in the criterion above imply that

S^{\prime}\in\mathsf{S}
.

Dually, an object
X\in\mathsf{T}
is
coapproachable to
\mathsf{S}

via
\mathsf{Y}
if every morphism
X\longrightarrow S
in
\mathsf{T}
with
an object
S\in\mathsf{S}
factorizes through an object from
\mathsf{Y}
.
The following lemma constitutes a standard technique for proving
full-and-faithfulness of triangulated functors induced by inclusions.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category and
\mathsf{S}
,
\mathsf{X}\subset\mathsf{T}

be two (strictly) full triangulated subcategories.
Let
\mathsf{Y}\subset\mathsf{S}\cap\mathsf{X}
be a full triangulated subcategory in
the intersection.
Assume that all objects of
\mathsf{X}
are approachable from
\mathsf{S}

via
\mathsf{Y}
.
Then the triangulated functor between the Verdier quotient categories

\mathsf{S}/\mathsf{Y}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{T}/\mathsf{X}

induced by the inclusion of triangulated categories

\mathsf{S}\rightarrowtail\mathsf{T}
is fully faithful.

Proof.

Straightforward from the definitions.
∎

In the following three lemmas, we list the basic properties of
the class of all approachable objects.

Lemma 7.3
.

For any two full subcategories
\mathsf{S}
,
\mathsf{Y}\subset\mathsf{T}
, the class of
all objects approachable from
\mathsf{S}
via
\mathsf{Y}
is closed under
direct summands in
\mathsf{T}
.
If the full subcategory
\mathsf{Y}\subset\mathsf{T}
is closed under finite
direct sums, then so is the full subcategory of all objects
approachable from
\mathsf{S}
via
\mathsf{Y}
.

Proof.

This lemma does not depend on the assumption that
\mathsf{T}
is
triangulated, but holds in any additive category.
We omit the largely straightforward proof (see the proof of the next
Lemma
7.4
for some details).
∎

Lemma 7.4
.

(a)
Assume that all infinite products exist in
\mathsf{T}
and
the full subcategory
\mathsf{Y}\subset\mathsf{T}
is closed under products.
Then the full subcategory of all objects approachable from
\mathsf{S}

via
\mathsf{Y}
is closed under products in
\mathsf{T}
.

(b)
Assume that all infinite coproducts exists in
\mathsf{T}
and
the full subcategory
\mathsf{Y}\subset\mathsf{T}
is closed under coproducts.
Then the full subcategory of all objects coapproachable to
\mathsf{S}

via
\mathsf{Y}
is closed under coproducts in
\mathsf{T}
.

Proof.

This lemma also does not depend on the assumption that
\mathsf{T}
is
triangulated.
Let us prove part (a) (part (b) is dual).
Let
X_{\alpha}
be a family of objects in
\mathsf{T}
such that
X_{\alpha}

is approachable from
\mathsf{S}
via
\mathsf{Y}
for every
\alpha
.
Let
S\longrightarrow\prod_{\alpha}X_{\alpha}
be a morphism into the product of

X_{\alpha}
in
\mathsf{T}
from an object
S\in\mathsf{S}
.
Let
S\longrightarrow X_{\alpha}
be the components of the morphism

S\longrightarrow\prod_{\alpha}X_{\alpha}
.
Then for every
\alpha
there exists an object
Y_{\alpha}\in\mathsf{Y}

such that the morphism
S\longrightarrow X_{\alpha}
factorizes
through
Y_{\alpha}
.
Hence the morphism
S\longrightarrow\prod_{\alpha}X_{\alpha}
factorizes through
the object
\prod_{\alpha}Y_{\alpha}\in\mathsf{Y}
.
∎

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category and
\mathsf{S}
,
\mathsf{Y}\subset\mathsf{T}

be two full triangulated subcategories such that
\mathsf{Y}\subset\mathsf{S}
.
Then the full subcategory of all objects approachable from
\mathsf{S}

via
\mathsf{Y}
is triangulated, that is, closed under shifts and cones
in
\mathsf{T}
.

Proof.

First of all, the class of all approachable objects is preserved
by the shifts in
\mathsf{T}
, because the full subcategories

\mathsf{S}
,
\mathsf{Y}\subset\mathsf{T}
are.

Furthermore, let
X\longrightarrow W\longrightarrow Z\longrightarrow X[1]
be a distinguished
triangle in
\mathsf{T}
with approachable objects
X
and
Z
,
and let
S\longrightarrow W
be a morphism in
\mathsf{T}
from an object
S\in\mathsf{S}
.
Consider the composition
S\longrightarrow W\longrightarrow Z
in
\mathsf{T}
.
By assumption, there exists an object
S^{\prime}\in\mathsf{T}
and a morphism

S^{\prime}\longrightarrow S
with a cone belonging to
\mathsf{Y}
such that the composition

S^{\prime}\longrightarrow S\longrightarrow W\longrightarrow Z
vanishes in
\mathsf{T}
.

Hence the composition
S^{\prime}\longrightarrow S\longrightarrow W
factorizes through
the morphism
X\longrightarrow W
in
\mathsf{T}
; so we obtain a morphism

S^{\prime}\longrightarrow X
.
Since
\mathsf{S}\subset\mathsf{T}
is a full triangulated subcategory and

\cone(S^{\prime}\to S)\in\mathsf{Y}\subset\mathsf{S}
, we have
S^{\prime}\in\mathsf{S}
.

By assumption, there exists an object
S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathsf{T}
(in fact,

S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathsf{S}
) and a morphism
S^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow S^{\prime}
with a cone belonging
to
\mathsf{Y}
such that the composition
S^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow S^{\prime}\longrightarrow X
vanishes.
Now, since
\mathsf{Y}\subset\mathsf{T}
is a full triangulated subcategory,
the composition
S^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow S^{\prime}\longrightarrow S
has a cone belonging to
\mathsf{Y}
.
The composition
S^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow S^{\prime}\longrightarrow S\longrightarrow W
vanishes in

\mathsf{T}
, and we are done.
∎

7.3.
Three lemmas

Let
\mathbf{A}
be a DG-category with shifts and cones, and let

U\overset{j}{\longrightarrow}V\overset{k}{\longrightarrow}W
be a three-term complex
in the additive category of closed morphisms
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
.
Denote by
T=\Tot(U\to V\to W)
be the corresponding total object
in
\mathbf{A}
.

By the definition, for any object
A\in\mathbf{A}
, the complex of morphisms

\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(A,T)
is naturally isomorphic to the totalization of
the bicomplex with three rows
\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(A,U)\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(A,V)\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(A,W)
.
In particular, for any
n\in\Gamma
, elements of the abelian group

\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{n}(A,T)
can be represented by triples
(f,g,h)
, where

f\in\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{n+1}(A,U)
,
g\in\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{n}(A,V)
, and

h\in\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{n-1}(A,W)
.

The following Lemmas
7.6

and
7.8
form a useful
technique for proving theorems about derived categories of
the second kind.

Lemma 7.6
.

(a)
The differential of an element of
\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{n}(A,T)

represented by a triple
(f,g,h)
is given by the rule

d(f,g,h)=(-df,-jf+dg,kg-dh).

(b)
Assume that
j=\ker(k)
in the additive category

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
.
Then a closed morphism
(f,g,h)\in\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{n}(A,T)
is homotopic to
zero whenever the morphism
h\in\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{n-1}(A,W)
can be lifted
to a morphism
t\in\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{n-1}(A,V)
, that is,
h=kt
.

(c)
Moreover, in the assumptions of (b), to any closed
morphism
(f,g,h)\in\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{n}(A,T)
and a lifting

t\in\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{n-1}(A,V)
of the morphism
h\in\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{n-1}(A,W)

one can assign a naturally defined contracting homotopy for
the morphism
(f,g,h)
.

Proof.

This is a straightforward generalization of
[
18
, Lemma 1.5.E]
.
Part (a) holds by the definition of the differential in a bicomplex
with three rows.

To prove part (b), one can observe that the identity inclusion
functor
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})\longrightarrow\mathbf{A}^{0}
preserves kernels, because
\mathbf{A}^{0}

is a full subcategory in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})

(see Lemma
3.9
) and
the functor
\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
preserves
kernels since it is a right adjoint.
So
0\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(A,U)\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(A,V)\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(A,W)
is a left exact sequence of complexes of abelian
groups.
Denoting by
\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(A,W)^{\prime}\subset\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(A,W)

the subcomplex constructed as the image of the map
\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(A,V)\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(A,W)
, we have a short exact sequence of
complexes
0\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(A,U)\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(A,V)\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(A,W)^{\prime}\longrightarrow 0
.
The total complex of this short exact sequence is an acyclic
subcomplex in
\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(A,T)
.
Hence any cocycle in
\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(A,T)
that belongs to this
subcomplex is a coboundary.

To prove part (c), one only needs to make the above proof of (b)
more explicit.
The equations
kt=h
,
dk=0
, and
kg-dh=0
imply that
k(dt-g)=d(kt)-kg=dh-kg=0
.
Since the short sequence of complexes of abelian groups in the proof
of part (b) is left exact, it follows that there exists a unique
element
s\in\Hom^{n}_{\mathbf{A}}(A,U)
such that
dt-g=js
.
Then the equations
dj=0
and
-jf+dg=0
imply that
jds=d(js)=-dg=-jf
; hence
ds=-f
and
d(s,t,0)=(f,g,h)
.
So
(s,t,0)
is the desired natural contracting homotopy.
∎

Let
H^{*}
be a graded abelian group.
As a particular case of the notation in
Proposition
3.1
and
Example
3.16
, we denote by
G^{-}(H^{*})

the contractible complex of abelian groups with the grading
components
G^{-}(H^{*})^{n}=H^{n}\oplus H^{n+1}
and the differential

d_{G^{-},n}\colon H^{n}\oplus H^{n+1}\longrightarrow H^{n+1}\oplus H^{n+2}

whose only nonzero component is the identity map

H^{n+1}\longrightarrow H^{n+1}
.
Notice that there is an obvious natural split epimorphism of graded
abelian groups
G^{-}(H^{*})\longrightarrow H^{*}
.

Recall the notation
\Hom_{\mathcal{Z}(\mathbf{B})}^{*}(X,Y)
for the graded abelian
group of cocycles in the complex of morphisms
\Hom_{\mathbf{B}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(X,Y)

between two objects
X
and
Y
in a DG-category
\mathbf{B}
.
Given two objects
X^{\natural}
and
Y^{\natural}\in\mathbf{A}^{\natural}
, denote by

\Hom_{\mathcal{Z}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})}^{-*}(X^{\natural},Y^{\natural})
the graded abelian group

\Hom_{\mathcal{Z}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})}^{*}(X^{\natural},Y^{\natural})
with the sign of the grading
inverted, i. e., the component of degree
n
in the former graded
abelian group is the component of degree
-n
in the latter one.

Lemma 7.7
.

For any DG-category
\mathbf{A}
with shifts and cones, and any objects

X^{\natural}\in\mathbf{A}^{\natural}
and
A\in\mathbf{A}
, there is a natural isomorphism of
complexes of abelian groups

(30)

\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{A}}(X^{\natural}),A)\simeq G^{-}(\Hom_{\mathcal{Z}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})}^{-*}(X^{\natural},\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}(A))).

Proof.

Recall the notation
X^{\natural}=(X,\sigma_{X})
for a generic object
in
\mathbf{A}^{\natural}
(see Section
3.2
).
By the definition, one has
\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{A}}(X^{\natural})=X
.

The object
E^{\natural}=\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}(A)\in\mathbf{A}^{\natural}
is constructed as

E^{\natural}=(E,\sigma_{E})
with
E=\cone(\mathrm{id}_{A}[-1])
and
\sigma_{E}=\iota^{\prime}\pi^{\prime}
(see Lemma
3.4
).
For any object
B\in\mathbf{A}
, the complex of morphisms
\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(B,E)

is naturally isomorphic to the cone of the identity endomorphism of
the complex
\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(B,A)[-1]
.
In other words, the elements of the abelian group
\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{n}(B,E)

are pairs
(g_{n-1},g_{n})
with the
g_{n-1}\in\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{n-1}(B,A)

and
g_{n}\in\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{n}(B,A)
, and the differential
d\colon\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{n}(B,E)\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{n+1}(B,A)
is given by the formula
d(g_{n-1},g_{n})=(-dg_{n-1}+g_{n},dg_{n})
.

The subgroup
\Hom^{-n}_{\mathcal{Z}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})}(X^{\natural},E^{\natural})\subset\Hom^{n}_{\mathbf{A}}(X,E)
consists of all the pairs
g=(g_{n-1},g_{n})
with

g_{n-1}\in\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{n-1}(X,A)
and
g_{n}\in\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{n}(X,A)

satisfying the equations
dg=0
and
d^{\natural}g=\sigma_{E}g-(-1)^{n}g\sigma_{X}=0
.
Explicitly, we have
\sigma_{E}(g_{n-1},g_{n})=(0,g_{n-1})
and

(g_{n-1},g_{n})\sigma_{X}=(g_{n-1}\sigma_{X},g_{n}\sigma_{X})
;
so the equation
\sigma_{E}g=(-1)^{n}g\sigma_{X}
means that

g_{n-1}\sigma_{X}=0
and
g_{n}\sigma_{X}=(-1)^{n}g_{n-1}
.
Assuming that
dg=0
, that is
g_{n}=dg_{n-1}
, we have

d(g_{n-1}\sigma_{X})=g_{n}\sigma_{X}+(-1)^{n-1}g_{n-1}d(\sigma_{X})=g_{n}\sigma_{X}+(-1)^{n-1}g_{n-1}
; so the equation
g_{n-1}\sigma_{X}=0

implies
g_{n}\sigma_{X}=(-1)^{n}g_{n-1}
.
We have computed the group
\Hom^{-n}_{\mathcal{Z}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})}(X^{\natural},E^{\natural})

as the subgroup in
\Hom^{n-1}_{\mathbf{A}}(X,A)
consisting of all the elements

g_{n-1}
such that
g_{n-1}\sigma_{X}=0
.

Now the grading component

G^{-}(\Hom^{-*}_{\mathcal{Z}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})}(X^{\natural},E^{\natural}))^{n}
is the group
of all pairs
(g,h)
with
g\in\Hom^{n-1}(X,A)
and
h\in\Hom^{n}(X,A)

such that
g\sigma_{X}=0=h\sigma_{X}
.
The differential on the complex

G^{-}(\Hom^{-n}_{\mathcal{Z}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})}(X^{\natural},E^{\natural}))
is given by the rule

d_{G^{-}}(g,h)=(h,0)
.
Finally, the desired isomorphism
\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(X,A)\longrightarrow G^{-}(\Hom_{\mathcal{Z}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})}^{-*}(X^{\natural},E^{\natural}))
is provided by
the map taking an element
f\in\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{n}(X,A)
to the pair
(g,h)

with
g=f\sigma_{X}
and
h=d(f)\sigma_{X}
.
The inverse map takes a pair
(g,h)
to the element

f=(-1)^{n}dg+(-1)^{n+1}h
.
One easily computes that the two maps are mutually inverse and
commute with the differentials.
∎

Notice that, by Lemmas
3.9

and
3.11
, in the context of
Lemma
7.7
there is
a natural isomorphism of graded abelian groups

(31)

\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{*}(\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{A}}(X^{\natural}),A)\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{Z}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})}^{-*}(\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{A}}(X^{\natural}),\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}(A)).

The composition with the adjunction morphism
X^{\natural}\longrightarrow\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{A}}(X^{\natural})
provides a map of graded abelian groups

\Hom_{\mathcal{Z}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})}^{-*}(\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{A}}(X^{\natural}),\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}(A))\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Hom_{\mathcal{Z}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})}^{-*}(X^{\natural},\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}(A)).

On the other hand, we have the natural split epimorphism of graded
abelian groups

G^{-}(\Hom_{\mathcal{Z}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})}^{-*}(X^{\natural},\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}(A)))\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Hom_{\mathcal{Z}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})}^{-*}(X^{\natural},\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}(A)).

The two maps of graded abelian groups above form a commutative
rhombus diagram

(32)

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern
