in the abelian category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
,
and
h\in\Hom^{-1}_{\mathbf{A}}(B,J)
is a contracting homotopy for
the identity embedding
\iota_{B}\colon B\longrightarrow J
, that is
\iota_{B}=d(t)
.
The partial order on
P
is defined in the obvious way: we say that

(B,t_{B})\leq(C,t_{C})
in
P
if
B\subset C\subset J
and
t_{B}=t_{C}|_{B}
.

It is important to observe that, since directed colimits are exact
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
, the union of a chain of subobjects coincides with
their direct limit.
Moreover, the identity functor
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})\longrightarrow\mathbf{A}^{0}
preserves
direct limits (since so does the functor
\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
, which can be extended to a fully faithful
embedding
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\mathbf{A}}\colon\mathbf{A}^{0}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
; see
Lemma
3.9
).
Hence, interpreting the homotopies
t_{B}
as morphisms

B\longrightarrow J[-1]
in
\mathbf{A}^{0}
and using the direct limit property of
the union, one can show that any chain (linearly ordered subset)
in
P
has an upper bound.

By Zorn’s lemma, the poset
B
has a maximal element
(B,t_{B})
.
It suffices to prove that the subobject
B\subset J
cannot be
different from the whole of
J
.
Indeed, otherwise there exists a subobject
C\subset J
such

B\subset C
and the quotient object
C/B
is a nonzero Noetherian
object in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
.
It remains to show that the homotopy
t_{B}\in\Hom^{-1}_{\mathbf{A}}(B,J)

can be extended to a compatible homotopy
t_{C}\in\Hom^{-1}_{\mathbf{A}}(C,J)
,
i. e., an element
t_{C}
can be chosen so that
d(t_{C})=\iota_{C}

and
t_{B}=t_{C}|_{B}
.

Consider the element
\widetilde{\Phi}(t_{B})\in\Hom_{\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})}(\Phi(B),\Phi(J[-1]))
.
Since
\Phi(B)
is a subobject of
\Phi(C)
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
and
the object
\Phi(J[-1])=\Phi(J)[1]
is injective in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
,
the morphism
\widetilde{\Phi}(t_{B})
can be extended to a morphism

s^{\prime}_{C}\in\Hom_{\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})}(\Phi(C),\Phi(J[-1]))
.
Since the functor
\widetilde{\Phi}
is fully faithful, we have
an element
t^{\prime}_{C}\in\Hom^{0}_{\mathbf{A}}(C,J[-1])\simeq\Hom^{-1}_{\mathbf{A}}(C,J)

such that
s^{\prime}_{C}=\widetilde{\Phi}(t^{\prime}_{C})
.
Simply put, we can extend our morphism
t_{B}\in\Hom^{-1}_{\mathbf{A}}(B,J)
to
some morphism
t^{\prime}_{C}\in\Hom^{-1}_{\mathbf{A}}(C,J)
such that
t_{B}=t^{\prime}_{C}|_{B}
.

Now the inclusion
B\rightarrowtail C
is a morphism in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
,
so it is a closed morphism in
\mathbf{A}
.
Therefore, we have
d(t^{\prime}_{C})|_{B}=d(t^{\prime}_{C}|_{B})=d(t_{B})=\iota_{B}
.
Hence, the difference
\iota_{C}-d(t^{\prime}_{C})
vanishes in restriction
to
B
.
Furthermore, we have
d(\iota_{C}-d(t^{\prime}_{C}))=d(\iota_{C})-d^{2}(t^{\prime}_{C})=0
;
so
\iota_{C}-d(t^{\prime}_{C})\colon C\longrightarrow J
is a morphism in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})

vanishing in restriction to
B
.

Denote by
E
the quotient object
C/B
in the abelian category

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
; so
E\in\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{fg}}
.
Let
\pi\colon C\longrightarrow E
be the natural epimorphism in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
.
Then there exists a morphism
f\colon E\longrightarrow J
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})

such that
\iota_{C}-d(t^{\prime}_{C})=f\pi
.
By the assumption of the lemma, the morphism
f
must be homotopic
to zero in
\mathbf{A}
; so there is a homotopy
t_{E}\in\Hom_{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}(E,J)

such that
d(t_{E})=f
.
Finally, we have
\iota_{C}=d(t^{\prime}_{C})+f\pi=d(t^{\prime}_{C}+t_{E}\pi)
and

(t^{\prime}_{C}+t_{E}\pi)|_{B}=t^{\prime}_{C}|_{B}+t_{E}\pi|_{B}=t_{B}
.
It remains to put
t_{C}=t^{\prime}_{C}+t_{E}\pi
.
∎

Remark 9.25
.

Let us warn the reader that
Theorem
9.23
does
not

completely describe the full triangulated subcategory of compact
objects in the compactly generated triangulated category
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{A})
.
Rather, the theorem claims that the full triangulated subcategory

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{fg}})
consists of compact objects and generates
the triangulated category
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{A})
.
It follows that the full triangulated subcategory of compact objects
in
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{A})
is the thick closure (coinciding with
the idempotent completion) of
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{fg}})
, that is,
any compact object of
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{A})
is a
direct summand
of
an object coming from
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{fg}})

[
30
, Lemma 2.2]
.

9.9.
Examples

Let us formulate here the particular cases of
Theorem
9.23
arising in
the context of Examples
9.5

and
9.12
.

The following well-known result is due to
Krause
[
27
, Proposition 2.3]
.

Corollary 9.26
.

Let
\mathsf{A}
be a locally Noetherian Grothendieck abelian category
and
\mathsf{A}_{\mathsf{fg}}\subset\mathsf{A}
be its full abelian subcategory of
Noetherian objects.
Then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{A}_{\mathsf{fg}})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{A})

induced by the inclusion of abelian categories
\mathsf{A}_{\mathsf{fg}}\rightarrowtail\mathsf{A}
is fully faithful, and its essential image coincides with
the full subcategory of compact objects in a compactly generated
triangulated category
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{A})
.

Proof.

The notation
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{B})
for the bounded derived category of
an exact/abelian category
\mathsf{B}
presumes that the complexes are graded
by the usual grading group of integers
\Gamma=\mathbb{Z}
.
To obtain this corollary as a particular case of
Theorem
9.23
one observes,
first of all, that the abelian category of unbounded complexes

\mathsf{C}(\mathsf{A})
is locally Noetherian for any locally Noetherian abelian
category
\mathsf{A}
.
The Noetherian objects of the category of complexes
\mathsf{C}(\mathsf{A})
are
the bounded complexes of Noetherian objects in
\mathsf{A}
, that is

\mathsf{C}(\mathsf{A})_{\mathsf{fg}}=\mathsf{C}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{A}_{\mathsf{fg}})
.

Furthermore, for any abelian category
\mathsf{B}
one can consider
the exactly embedded full abelian DG-subcategory of bounded complexes
in
\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{B})
, denoted by
\mathbf{C}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{B})\subset\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{B})
.
Then the absolute derived category
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{C}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{B}))
can be
easily seen to agree with the bounded derived category of
\mathsf{B}
,
i. e.,
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{C}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{B}))=\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{B})

(cf. Lemma
5.9

or
[
38
, Section A.1]
).
In particular,
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{C}(\mathsf{A})_{\mathbf{fg}})=\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{A}_{\mathsf{fg}})
.

Finally, Theorem
9.23
still
gives a weaker result since is leaves the question of direct summands
open (as per Remark
9.25
).
To deduce the full assertion formulated in the corollary, one needs
to check that the bounded derived category
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{B})
is
idempotent-complete for any abelian category
\mathsf{B}
.
A more general result, for idempotent-complete exact categories
rather than abelian ones, can be found in
[
6
, Theorem 2.8]
.
∎

The next assertion can be found in
[
35
, Theorems 3.11.1
and 3.11.2]
.

Corollary 9.27
.

Let
\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(R^{*},d,h)
be a CDG-ring such that the graded ring

R^{*}
is graded left Noetherian.
Let
\mathbf{A}=\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}}
be the locally Noetherian DG-category of
left CDG-modules over
\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, and let

\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{fg}}=\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}}_{\mathbf{fg}}\subset\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}}
be the exactly
embedded full abelian DG-subcategory whose objects are
the CDG-modules with Noetherian underlying graded
R^{*}
-modules.
Then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}}_{\mathbf{fg}})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}})

induced by the inclusion of abelian DG-categories
\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}}_{\mathbf{fg}}\rightarrowtail\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}}
is fully faithful, and the objects in
its image form a set of compact generators of the coderived
category
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}})
.

Proof.

Notice that, by
Lemmas
9.6
–
9.7
or by
Lemma
9.10
, a left
CDG-module
(M^{*},d_{M})
over
(R^{*},d,h)
is Noetherian as
an object of
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}})
if and only if the graded

R^{*}
-module
M^{*}
is Noetherian as an object of the category
of graded modules
R^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
.
The rest is explained in Example
9.12
(1)
and Theorem
9.23
.
∎

The following corollary is a generalization
of
[
18
, Proposition 1.5(d)]
.

Corollary 9.28
.

Let
X
be a quasi-compact quasi-separated scheme and

\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B^{*},d,h)
be a quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebra over
X

such that the quasi-coherent graded quasi-algebra
B^{*}
is graded
left Noetherian.
Let
\mathbf{A}=\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--qcoh}}}
be the locally Noetherian DG-category of
quasi-coherent left CDG-modules over
\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, and let
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{fg}}=\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--qcoh}}}_{\mathbf{fg}}\subset\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--qcoh}}}
be the exactly embedded full abelian
DG-subcategory whose objects are the CDG-modules with Noetherian
underlying quasi-coherent graded
B^{*}
-modules.
Then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--qcoh}}}_{\mathbf{fg}})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--qcoh}}})

induced by the inclusion of abelian DG-categories
\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--qcoh}}}_{\mathbf{fg}}\rightarrowtail\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--qcoh}}}
is fully faithful, and the objects in
its image form a set of compact generators of the coderived
category
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--qcoh}}})
.

Proof.

Similarly to the proof of
Corollary
9.27
, the same
lemmas imply that a quasi-coherent CDG-module
(M^{*},d_{M})
over

(B^{*},d,h)
is Noetherian as an object of
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\boldsymbol{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--qcoh}}})
if and
only if the quasi-coherent graded
B^{*}
-module
M^{*}
is Noetherian as
an object of the category of quasi-coherent graded modules
B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--qcoh}}}
.
Then it remains to refer to
Example
9.12
(2) and
Theorem
9.23
.
∎

The next corollary generalizes
[
18
, Corollary 2.3(l)]
.

Corollary 9.29
.

Let
\mathsf{A}
be a locally Noetherian abelian category and

\Lambda\colon\mathsf{A}\longrightarrow\mathsf{A}
be an autoequivalence.
Assume that either
\Gamma=\mathbb{Z}
, or
\Lambda
is involutive
and
\Gamma=\mathbb{Z}/2
.
Let
w\colon\mathrm{Id}_{\mathsf{A}}\longrightarrow\Lambda^{2}
be a potential (as in
Section
2.5
).
Then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{A}_{\mathsf{fg}},\Lambda,w))\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w))

induced by the inclusion of abelian categories
\mathsf{A}_{\mathsf{fg}}\rightarrowtail\mathsf{A}
is fully faithful, and the objects in its image form a set of
compact generators of the coderived category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w)
.

Proof.

Here, as usual, we denote the restrictions of
\Lambda
and
w

to
\mathsf{A}_{\mathsf{fg}}\subset\mathsf{A}
simply by
\Lambda
and
w
.
First of all, we observe that the abelian DG-category of
factorizations
\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w)
is locally Noetherian whenever
an abelian category
\mathsf{A}
is locally Noetherian.
Using, e. g.,
Lemmas
9.6
–
9.7
or
Lemma
9.10
, one shows that
the full DG-subcategory of Noetherian objects

\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w)_{\mathbf{fg}}\subset\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w)
is
the abelian DG-category of factorizations in
\mathsf{A}_{\mathsf{fg}}
, that is

\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{A},\Lambda,w)_{\mathbf{fg}}=\mathbf{F}(\mathsf{A}_{\mathsf{fg}},\Lambda,w)
.
Then it remains to apply
Theorem
9.23
.
∎

9.10.
Fp-injective and fp-projective objects

We start the discussion in the context of locally finitely presentable
categories before passing to the better behaved special case of
locally coherent categories.
Recall from the discussion in
Section
9.2
that any locally
finitely generated abelian category is Grothendieck (a more general
result can be found in
[
49
, Corollary 9.6]
); in particular,
any locally finitely presentable abelian category is Grothendieck.
For a discussion of fp-injective and fp-projective modules over
a ring, in a context closely related to our exposition in this paper,
see
[
41
, Section 1]
and the references therein.

Let
\mathsf{A}
be a locally finitely presentable abelian category.
An object
J\in\mathsf{A}
is said to be
fp-injective
if

\Ext_{\mathsf{A}}^{1}(E,J)=0
for all finitely presentable objects
E\in\mathsf{A}
.
An object
P\in\mathsf{A}
is said to be
fp-projective
if

\Ext_{\mathsf{A}}^{1}(P,J)=0
for all fp-injective objects
J\in\mathsf{A}
.
We will denote the full subcategories of fp-injective and
fp-projective objects by
\mathsf{A}_{\mathsf{fpinj}}
and
\mathsf{A}_{\mathsf{fproj}}\subset\mathsf{A}
,
respectively.

Notice that the condition
\Ext_{\mathsf{A}}^{1}(S,J)=0
for all
finitely
generated
objects in a locally finitely generated abelian category

\mathsf{A}
implies injectivity of the object
J
.
Hence in a locally Noetherian category
\mathsf{A}
, where all finitely
generated objects are finitely presentable, one has that all
fp-injective objects are injective and all objects are fp-projective.
This is
not
true in a locally coherent category which is not
locally Noetherian.
For locally coherent categories, fp-injectivity and fp-projectivity
are important technical concepts.

Let
\mathsf{A}
be a Grothendieck category and
A\in\mathsf{A}
be an object.
An (ordinal-indexed increasing)
filtration

F
on
A
is
a family of subobjects
F_{i}\subset A
, indexed by the ordinals

0\leq i\leq\alpha
, where
\alpha
is a fixed ordinal, and satisfying
the following conditions:

•

F_{0}=0
and
F_{\alpha}=A
;

•

F_{i}\subset F_{j}
for all ordinals
i\leq j\leq\alpha
;

•

F_{j}=\bigcup_{i<j}F_{i}
for all limit ordinals
j\leq\alpha
.

Given a filtration
F
on an object
A
, one says that the object
A

is
filtered by
the quotient objects
F_{i+1}/F_{i}
, where

0\leq i<\alpha
.
In an alternative terminology, the object
A
is said to be
a
transfinitely iterated extension
(
in the sense of
the directed colimit
) of the objects
F_{i+1}/F_{i}\in\mathsf{A}
.
Notice that, in particular, an infinite coproduct is a transfinitely
iterated extension.

Proposition 9.30
.

Let
\mathsf{A}
be a locally finitely generated abelian category.

(a)
For any object
A\in\mathsf{A}
there exists a short exact
sequence

0\longrightarrow J^{\prime}\longrightarrow P\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow 0

in
\mathsf{A}
with an fp-injective object
J^{\prime}
and an fp-projective
object
P
.

(b)
For any object
A\in\mathsf{A}
there exists a short exact
sequence

0\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow J\longrightarrow P^{\prime}\longrightarrow 0

in
\mathsf{A}
with an fp-injective object
J
and an fp-projective
object
P^{\prime}
.

(c)
An object
P\in\mathsf{A}
is fp-projective if and only if it is
a direct summand of an object filtered by finitely presentable objects.

Proof.

This is a version of a classical theorem of Eklof and
Trlifaj
[
19
]
.
For a generalization covering the situation at hand, see,
e. g.,
[
52
, Theorems 3.3 and 3.4]
.
It is important here that there are enough fp-injective objects
in
\mathsf{A}
, in the sense that any object is a subobject of
an fp-injective one (and in fact, even a subobject of an injective
object), and there are enough fp-projective objects, in the sense
that any object is a quotient of an fp-projective object.
Indeed, coproducts of fp-projective objects are easily seen to
be fp-projective; in particular, coproducts of finitely presentable
objects are fp-projective; and any object in a locally finitely
presentable category is a quotient of a coproduct of finitely
presentable ones.
∎

Lemma 9.31
.

Let
\mathsf{A}
be a locally finitely presentable abelian category.
Then

(a)
both the classes of fp-injective and fp-projective
objects in
\mathsf{A}
are closed under extensions and direct summands;

(b)
the class of all fp-projective objects in
\mathsf{A}
is
closed under transfinitely iterated extensions;

(c)
both the classes of fp-projective and fp-injective
objects in
\mathsf{A}
are closed under coproducts.
The class of all fp-injective objects is also closed under products.

Proof.

Part (a) follows immediately from the definitions.
Part (b) is a version of a classical

Eklof lemma

[
19
, Lemma 1]
and essentially a part of
Proposition
9.30
(cf.
[
52
, Proposition 1.3]
).
In part (c), the coproducts of fp-projective objects are
fp-projective and the products of fp-injective objects are
fp-injective by a general property of
\Ext^{1}
-orthogonal
classes in abelian categories with (co)products.

The fact that coproducts of fp-injective objects are fp-injective
is a property of right
\Ext^{1}
-orthogonal clases to sets of
finitely presentable objects in locally finitely presentable
categories.
Notice that, for any finitely presentable object
E\in\mathsf{A}
and
any object
B\in\mathsf{A}
, the group
\Ext^{1}_{\mathsf{A}}(E,B)
can be computed
as the filtered colimit of cokernels of the restriction maps

\Hom_{\mathsf{A}}(F,B)\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathsf{A}}(G,B)
taken over all the short exact
sequences
0\longrightarrow G\longrightarrow F\longrightarrow E\longrightarrow 0
in
\mathsf{A}
with
a
finitely presentable
object
F
.
Furthermore, the cokernel
G
of any epimorphism of finitely
presentable objects
F\longrightarrow E
is a finitely generated (hence
small) object.
∎

Lemma 9.32
.

Let
\mathsf{A}
be a locally coherent abelian category. Then

(a)
the class of all fp-injective objects in
\mathsf{A}

is closed under directed colimits;

(b)
the class of all fp-injective objects in
\mathsf{A}

is closed under the cokernels of monomorphisms;

(c)
the class of all fp-projective objects in
\mathsf{A}

is closed under the kernels of epimorphisms;

(d)
one has
\Ext_{\mathsf{A}}^{n}(P,J)=0
for any fp-projective
object
P\in\mathsf{A}
, any fp-injective object
J\in\mathsf{A}
, and all
integers
n\geq 1
.

Proof.

The proof of part (a) is similar to the second paragraph of
the proof of
Lemma
9.31
above;
one only needs to observe that in a locally coherent category

\mathsf{A}
the object
G
is finitely presentable (coherent), too.
Parts (b–d) are equivalent expressions of the assertion that

the fp cotorsion pair is hereditary
in a locally
coherent category (see
[
52
, Lemma 1.4]
for the general
theory) and follow from the same observation as part (a).
∎

Notice that
Lemmas
9.31
(a)
and
9.32
(b–c) and
the observations in the proof of Proposition
9.30

imply, in particular, that the class of all fp-projective objects
is resolving, while the class of all fp-injective objects is
coresolving in a locally coherent category
\mathsf{A}
.
Hence one can speak about the
fp-projective
(resolution)
and the
fp-injective
(coresolution)
dimensions
of
objects of
\mathsf{A}
.

Lemma 9.33
.

Given a locally coherent category
\mathsf{A}
and an integer
n\geq 0
,
the following two conditions are equivalent:

(1)

all the objects of
\mathsf{A}
have fp-projective dimensions
not exceeding
n
;

(2)

all the fp-injective objects in
\mathsf{A}
have injective
dimensions not exceeding
n
.

Proof.

Both the conditions are equivalent to vanishing of the Ext groups

\Ext^{i}_{\mathbf{A}}(A,J)
with
i>n
for all objects
A\in\mathsf{A}
and

J\in\mathsf{A}_{\mathsf{fpinj}}
.
∎

We will say that the
fp-projective dimension
of a locally
coherent category
\mathsf{A}
is
\leq n
if the equivalent conditions
of Lemma
9.33
hold.
In particular, a locally coherent category has fp-projective
dimension zero if and only if it is locally Noetherian.

Lemma 9.34
.

Let
\mathsf{E}
and
\mathsf{F}
be exact categories, and let
\Phi\colon\mathsf{E}\longrightarrow\mathsf{F}
and
\Psi\colon\mathsf{F}\longrightarrow\mathsf{E}
be an adjoint pair of
exact functors, with the functor
\Psi
right adjoint to
\Phi
.
Then for any two objects
E\in\mathsf{E}
and
F\in\mathsf{F}
there are natural
isomorphisms of Yoneda Ext groups

\Ext^{n}_{\mathsf{E}}(E,\Psi(F))\simeq\Ext^{n}_{\mathsf{F}}(\Phi(E),F)\qquad\text{for all \ $n\geq 0$.}

Proof.

One can, e. g., show that the functors induced by
\Phi
and
\Psi

on the (bounded or unbounded) derived categories of
\mathsf{E}
and
\mathsf{F}

are adjoint, by constructing the natural morphisms of adjunction unit
and counit for complexes and noticing that the required equations for
the compositions hold.
Then the desired isomorphism of the Ext groups follows.
∎

Lemma 9.35
.

Let
\mathbf{A}
be a locally coherent DG-category. Then

(a)
both the additive functors
\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
and
\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{A}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})

preserve fp-injectivity of objects;

(b)
both the functors also preserve fp-projectivity of
objects.

Proof.

Using Lemma
9.34
for
n=1
, part (a)
follows from the fact that both the functors preserve finite
presentability of objects
(Lemma
9.6
), and
part (b) follows from part (a).
Here, as usual, one should keep in mind that the functor

\Psi^{-}_{\mathbf{A}}
right adjoint to
\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}
only differs from
the functor
\Psi^{+}_{\mathbf{A}}
by a shift,
\Psi^{-}(X)=\Psi^{+}(X)[1]
.
∎

Let
\mathbf{A}
be a locally coherent (Grothendieck abelian) DG-category.
An object
J\in\mathbf{A}
is said to be
graded-fp-injective
if

\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}(J)\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
is an fp-injective object of
the abelian category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
.
We will denote the full DG-subcategory of graded-fp-injective
objects by
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{fpinj}}\subset\mathbf{A}
.
The following lemma is similar to Lemma
5.4
.

Lemma 9.36
.

For any locally coherent DG-category
\mathbf{A}
, the full DG-subcategory
of graded-fp-injective objects
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{fpinj}}\subset\mathbf{A}
is additive
and closed under shifts and twists (hence also under cones) as well as
under direct summands, infinite products, and infinite coproducts.
The full DG-subcategory
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{fpinj}}
inherits an exact DG-category
structure from the abelian exact DG-category structure of
\mathbf{A}
.
The inclusion
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{fpinj}}\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}((\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{fpinj}})^{\natural})
holds in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{L}=\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{fpinj}}
be the full subcategory of
fp-injective objects in the locally coherent abelian category

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
.
The full subcategory
\mathsf{L}
is additive, closed under extensions,
direct summands, and the cokernels of monomorphisms in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})

(Lemmas
9.31
(a)
and
9.32
(b)), and
preserved by the shift functors acting on
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
.
By Proposition
4.27
(a–d), all the assertions
of the lemma follow except the ones concerning infinite products
and coproducts.
To prove these, one recalls that the full subcategory

\mathsf{L}=\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{fpinj}}\subset\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
is also closed
under products and coproducts
(Lemma
9.31
(c)),
and the functor
\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}
preserves products and coproducts.
∎

9.11.
Compact generation:
\aleph_{n}
-Noetherian coherent case

The following proposition is a generalization
of
[
41
, Theorem 2.2]
.

Proposition 9.37
.

Let
\mathbf{A}
be a locally coherent DG-category.
Then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{fpinj}})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{A})

induced by the inclusion of exact/abelian DG-categories
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{fpinj}}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{A}
is an equivalence of triangulated categories.

Proof.

Apply Theorem
7.11
(b) to
the exact DG-subpair
(\mathbf{G},\mathsf{M})=(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{fpinj}},\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{fpinj}})

in the exact DG-pair
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})=(\mathbf{A},\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural}))
.
To check the assumptions, recall that any abelian DG-category has
twists by Proposition
4.35
.
Furthermore, the class of all fp-injective objects is coresolving
in a locally coherent abelian category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
according to
Lemmas
9.31
(a)
and
9.32
(b), and
because there are enough injective (hence enough fp-injective) objects.
∎

The next proposition is a generalization
of
[
41
, Theorem 2.4]
.
It is also a generalization of
Proposition
9.22
above.

Proposition 9.38
.

Let
\mathbf{A}
be a locally coherent DG-category.
Assume that the locally coherent abelian category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})

has finite fp-projective dimension.
Then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{inj}})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{A})

induced by the inclusion of exact/abelian DG-categories
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{inj}}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{A}
is an equivalence of triangulated categories.

Proof.

The point is that condition (
*
) is satisfied for locally coherent
abelian categories of finite fp-projective dimension, and consequently
Theorem
5.10
(a)
is applicable.
Indeed, in a locally coherent category of finite fp-projective
dimension, all coproducts of injective objects are fp-injective
by Lemma
9.31
(c),
and all fp-injective objects have finite injective dimensions
by the definition.
The rest was already explained in the proof of
Proposition
9.22
.
∎

The following theorem is our version of
[
58
, Corollary 6.13]

and
[
41
, Corollary 2.6]
.
It is also a generalization of
Theorem
9.23
above.

Theorem 9.39
.

Let
\mathbf{A}
be a locally coherent DG-category and

\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{fp}}\subset\mathbf{A}
be its full DG-subcategory of finitely
presentable (coherent) objects.
Assume that
both
the locally coherent abelian categories

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
and
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
have finite fp-projective
dimensions.
Then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{fp}})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{A})

induced by the inclusion of abelian DG-categories

\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{fp}}\longrightarrow\mathbf{A}
is fully faithful, and the objects in its image
form a set of compact generators of the coderived category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{A})
.

Proof.

To prove the full-and-faithfulness of the triangulated inclusion
functor and compactness of all the objects in its essential image,
we once again apply
Theorem
9.21
.
Consider the exact DG-pair
(\mathbf{E},\mathsf{K})=(\mathbf{A},\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural}))
and its
exact DG-subpair
(\mathbf{F},\mathsf{L})=(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{fp}},\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural}_{\mathbf{fp}}))

(recall that
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural}_{\mathbf{fp}})=\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})_{\mathsf{fp}}
, according to
the discussion in Section
9.5
).
Recall further that all finitely generated (hence all finitely
presentable) objects are small, and observe that the full subcategory
of coherent objects is self-resolvable in any locally coherent category.

In order to prove that the coherent objects generate the coderived
category
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{A})
, we use the result of
Proposition
9.38
,
telling that any object of
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{A})
can be represented by
a graded-injective object of
\mathbf{A}
under the assumption of finite
fp-projective dimension of
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
.
The semiorthogonality assertion of
Theorem
5.5
(a) implies
that the Verdier quotient functor
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{A})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{A})

induces an isomorphism of the Hom groups
\Hom_{\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{A})}(A,J)\simeq\Hom_{\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbf{A})}(A,J)
for all objects
A\in\mathbf{A}
and

J\in\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{inj}}
.

Let
J\in\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{inj}}
be a graded-injective object such that all
closed morphisms
E\longrightarrow J
from coherent objects
E\in\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{fp}}

to
J
are homotopic to zero in
\mathbf{A}
.
We have to prove that the object
J
is contractible.
For this purpose, we will show that all closed morphisms
A\longrightarrow J

from an arbitrary object
A\in\mathbf{A}
to the object
J
are homotopic
to zero.

We start with picking an epimorphism
P_{0}\longrightarrow A
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})

with an fp-projective object
P_{0}
.
Denoting the kernel of this epimorphism by
A_{1}
, we choose
an epimorphism
P_{1}\longrightarrow A_{1}
with an fp-projective object
P_{1}
,
etc.
Proceeding in this way, we construct an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow Q\longrightarrow P_{n-1}\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow P_{0}\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow 0

in the abelian category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
, where
n
is the fp-projective
dimension of
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
.
Then the object
Q\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
is also fp-projective.
Put
P_{n}=Q
.

By Lemma
5.9
, the object

X=\Tot(P_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\to A)
is absolutely acyclic in
\mathbf{A}
.
Put
B=\Tot(P_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\in\mathbf{A}
.
Then we have a distinguished triangle
X\longrightarrow B\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow X[1]
in
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
.
Using
Theorem
5.5
(a) again,
we see that it suffices to show that all closed morphisms
B\longrightarrow J

are homotopic to zero in
\mathbf{A}
.

Now the object
B\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
is fp-projective as a finitely
iterated extension of fp-projective objects
(use the proof of Lemma
4.2
together with
Lemma
9.31
(a)
or (b)).
By Proposition
9.30
(c), the object
B\in\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})

is a direct summand of an object
F
filtered by coherent objects
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
.
Clearly, it is enough to show that all closed morphisms
F\longrightarrow J

are homotopic to zero in
\mathbf{A}
.

The following lemma, which is a rather straightforward generalization
of Arinkin’s Lemma
9.24
, finishes the proof of
the theorem.
∎

Lemma 9.40
.

Let
\mathbf{A}
be a locally finitely presentable abelian DG-category
and
J\in\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{inj}}
be a graded-injective object.
Assume that all closed morphisms
E\longrightarrow J
from finitely
presentable objects
E
of
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
to
J
are homotopic to zero
in
\mathbf{A}
.
Let
F\in\mathbf{A}
be an object filtered by finitely presentable objects
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
.
Then all closed morphisms
F\longrightarrow J
are also homotopic to zero
in
\mathbf{A}
.

Proof.

Instead of adopting the proof of Lemma
9.24
to
the new situation (which is quite easy to do), we will structure
the argument differently in order to demonstrate a connection
with the Eklof lemma.
The next lemma provides natural isomorphisms of abelian groups

\Ext^{1}_{\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})}(E,J[-1])\simeq\Hom_{\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{A})}(E,J)
and

\Ext^{1}_{\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})}(F,J[-1])\simeq\Hom_{\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{A})}(F,J)
.
The former isomorphism shows that the object
J
is fp-injective in

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
in our assumptions, and then the latter isomorphism
implies the desired vanishing of closed morphisms
F\longrightarrow J

up to homotopy in
\mathbf{A}
.
∎

Lemma 9.41
.

Let
\mathbf{E}
be an exact DG-category.
Then, for any two objects
A
and
B\in\mathbf{E}
, the kernel of
the map of abelian groups

(42)

\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}\colon\Ext^{1}_{\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})}(A,B)\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Ext^{1}_{\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})}(\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(A),\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}(B))

induced by the exact functor
\Phi_{\mathbf{E}}\colon\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\natural})
is naturally isomorphic to the group

\Hom_{\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})}(A,B[1])
.
In particular, if either
A
is a graded-projective object
in
\mathbf{E}
, or
B
is a graded-injective object in
\mathbf{E}
, then

\Ext^{1}_{\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})}(A,B)\simeq\Hom_{\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{A})}(A,B[1]).

Proof.

This is a quite well-known observation (formulated explicitly
for complexes in abelian categories in
[
52
, Lemma 5.1]

and for abelian DG-categories in
[
53
, Lemma 6.1]
).
The point is that the kernel of the map (
42
)
is the abelian group of equivalence classes of short sequences

0\longrightarrow B\longrightarrow C\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow 0
in
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
that are
split exact in
\mathbf{E}^{0}
(in view of
Lemma
3.9
).
Irrespectively of any exact structure on
\mathbf{E}
, in any DG-category
with shifts and cones such “graded split” short exact sequences
are indexed by the closed morphisms
f\colon A\longrightarrow B[1]
up to homotopy,
and the object
C
is recovered as
C=\cone(f)[-1]
(as was mentioned
already in Section
1.3
).
∎

Corollary 9.42
.

Let
\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(R^{*},d,h)
be a CDG-ring.
Assume that the graded ring
R^{*}
is graded left coherent and there
exists an integer
n\geq 0
such that all homogenenous left ideals in

R^{*}
have less than
\aleph_{n}
generators.
Let
\mathbf{A}=\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}}
be the locally coherent DG-category of
left CDG-modules over
\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, and let
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{fp}}=\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}}_{\mathbf{fp}}\subset\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}}
be the exactly embedded full abelian
DG-subcategory whose objects are the CDG-modules with coherent
(finitely presentable) underlying graded
R^{*}
-modules.
Then the triangulated functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}}(\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}}_{\mathbf{fp}})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}})

induced by the inclusion of abelian DG-categories
\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}}_{\mathbf{fp}}\rightarrowtail\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}}
is fully faithful, and the objects in
its image form a set of compact generators of the coderived
category
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}})
.

Proof.

According to Example
9.15
,
the abelian DG-category
\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}}
is locally coherent under our
assumptions.
Notice that, by
Lemmas
9.6
–
9.7
or by
Lemma
9.13
, a left CDG-module

(M^{*},d_{M})
over
(R^{*},d,h)
is coherent as an object of

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}})
if and only if the graded
R^{*}
-module
M^{*}

is coherent as an object of the category of graded modules
R^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--mod}}}
.

Let us explain how the
\aleph_{n}
-Noetherianity assumption in
the formulation of the corollary implies both the fp-projective
dimension assumptions of
Theorem
9.39
.
As stated in Example
9.12
(1), all
homogeneous left ideals in the graded ring
R^{*}[\delta]
have less
than
\aleph_{n}
generators if and only if all homogeneous left
ideals in the graded ring
R^{*}
have this property.
According to the graded version of
[
41
, Proposition 2.3]
,
the fp-projective dimension of the locally coherent category
of graded left
R^{*}
-modules
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}}^{\natural})
does not
exceed
n
in this case.
The same, of course, applies to the locally coherent category

\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\boldsymbol{R}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--mod}}})
of graded left modules over
R^{*}[\delta]
.

We have checked all the assumptions of
Theorem
9.39

in the situation at hand, and invoking this theorem proves
the corollary.
∎

Remark 9.43
.

The results of the paper
[
53
]
allow to drop the finite
fp-dimension conditions in
Theorem
9.39

and the
\aleph_{n}
-Noetherianity condition in
Corollary
9.42

at the cost of replacing the coderived category in the sense
of the present author (as defined in
Section
5.1
) by
the coderived category in the sense of Becker
(as defined in
[
53
, Section 7.3]
).
The respective results in
[
53
]
are
[
53
, Theorem 0.2
or 8.19]
and
[
53
, Corollary 0.3 or 8.20]
.

The terminology of Positselski’s vs. Becker’s co/contraderived
categories was established in
[
52
, Remark 9.2]

and
[
47
, Section 7.9]
.
It is an open problem whether the present author’s and Becker’s
approaches to defining coderived and contraderived categories

ever
produce different outputs within their common domain
of definition (see
[
43
, Examples 2.5(3) and 2.6(3)]
for
a discussion).

Let
\mathbf{A}
be a locally coherent DG-category.
Assuming that the locally coherent abelian category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})

has finite fp-projective dimension,
Proposition
9.38
tells
that Positselski’s and Becker’s coderived categories of
\mathbf{A}
agree.
So, even if one is interested the coderived category in the sense of
the present author, the methods of the paper
[
53
]
provide
an improvement over our results in that they imply that the condition
of finite fp-projective dimension of the category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A})
can be
dropped in
Theorem
9.39
,
and only for the category
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{\natural})
this condition is needed.
This conclusion is essentially based on
[
53
, Corollary 7.19]
,
which is a more powerful technique for proving that finitely presentable
objects generate the coderived category, as compared to our argument
in the proof of
Theorem
9.39
above.
